### C1.5 Universal Wealth Access

Pass Threshold (v2.0): ≥80% population with an active wealth-accumulation pathway. Revision class W (wording, commentary or disclosure only).

v2.0: 1 at 1.0, 12 at 0.5, 10 at 0.0. v1: 1 at 1.0, 11 at 0.5, 11 at 0.0.

**CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/cco.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> PTF acre equity provides near-universal wealth access (projected 80%+ participation), but full universality depends on implementation completeness that remains modeled rather than field-validated — consistent with this system's own general caveat elsewhere about modeling versus real-world confirmation.

**Nordic Social Democracy: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/nsd.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Approximately 70% have wealth accumulation pathways through homeownership programs, pension systems, and savings incentives — near-universal, non-gated reach, meeting this narrowed criterion's access-breadth threshold on the strength of institutional coverage rather than a numeric technicality. *(Unchanged from the legacy score — Paper Appendix H.7v2's own worked example notes this qualitative-tolerance judgment call "was never about the Gini clause," which has moved to C1.2b above.)*

**Market Socialism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/ms.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Members have wealth access, but require cooperative membership. Unemployed, between jobs, or unable to work lack access. Not truly universal across the entire population.

**Participatory Economics: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/pe.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Participatory councils provide universal economic *input* — access to the underlying mechanism is explicitly universal by design, since all participants are council members — but C1.2a's own finding that the mechanism's asset-growth power is only partial keeps this at partial credit rather than a full Pass, to avoid crediting universal access to a mechanism that isn't itself fully load-bearing.

**Integral: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/int.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> **STRUCTURAL FAILURE.** This narrowed criterion asks about access breadth to an asset-growth mechanism, not accumulation adequacy (C1.2a) or concentration prevention (C1.2b) — and Integral's universal contribution-recognition design means access would be about as broad as possible, if there were a mechanism to access. But C1.2a already establishes no such mechanism exists at all; access to a mechanism that builds no assets does not satisfy this criterion, following directly from C1.2a's own finding rather than a separate access-breadth problem.

**Sovereign Wealth Fund Statism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/swf.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> This evaluation follows the precedent Participatory Economics' own C1.5 write-up already establishes for exactly this situation (Report, System 11): "access to the underlying mechanism is explicitly universal by design... but C1.2a's own finding that the mechanism's asset-growth power is only partial keeps this at partial credit rather than a full Pass, to avoid crediting universal access to a mechanism that isn't itself fully load-bearing." Access here is, if anything, more cleanly universal than that precedent's own case: Alaska's PFD reaches essentially 100% of qualifying residents with no means test, employment condition, or membership gate of any kind (more than 600,000 recipients in 2025 alone) — a broader, less-gated reach than even Nordic Social Democracy's own ~70% pension/homeownership coverage. But since C1.2a lands at 0.5 rather than 1.0, this criterion tracks it downward for the same reason Participatory Economics' does. *(continues in the scoring document)*

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.0. Follows C1.2a

**Ostrom-Style Commons Governance: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/os.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> This is the criterion where the scale convention bites hardest, and it is a **bound rather than a shortfall**. Principle 1 — clearly defined boundaries, defining who may appropriate — makes exclusion **constitutive** of the mechanism. A commons that admits everyone is not an Ostrom-style commons; it is the open-access resource the whole design exists to prevent. Universality is therefore precluded by the first principle, not merely unachieved. The observed reach, up to 2.5 billion of roughly 8 billion people, is under a third, and the mechanism supplies no route to 80%. *(continues in the scoring document)*

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5.

**Universal Basic Income: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/ubi.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> **STRUCTURAL FAILURE.** Income is not wealth. UBI provides monthly survival but no ownership stakes, no asset accumulation, no economic power beyond consumption — following directly from C1.2a's own finding.

**Degrowth Economics: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/dg.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Commons-based wealth provides universal *access* to resources — community gardens, tool libraries, shared spaces — but personal wealth accumulation itself is limited by design, a different conception of wealth than this criterion assumes.

**Georgism / Land Value Tax (LVT + Citizen's Dividend): 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/geo.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> **STRUCTURAL FAILURE.** This narrowed criterion asks whether the population has broad *access* to an *active mechanism producing asset growth* — access breadth is arguably the one part of this pairing Georgism gets right (no membership gate, no employment requirement, no means test), but C1.2a's analysis already establishes that the dividend mechanism does not function as an asset-growth vehicle at all, for any participant, regardless of access breadth. Access to a mechanism that does not itself build assets does not satisfy this criterion's stated requirement — the same underlying reason as C1.2a, not an access-breadth problem.

**Islamic Finance / Profit-Sharing Banking: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/if.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Access to the accumulation vehicles is not membership-gated: anyone may open a Shariah-compliant savings or investment account, and a PricewaterhouseCoopers Malaysia study cited by the World Bank found that the majority of Islamic finance customers in Malaysia are non-Muslim — direct evidence that the mechanism does not gate on religious affiliation. Against that, the equity-based products are effectively absent at the bottom of the distribution: the literature reports that **no Malaysian microfinance institution offers *musharakah* or *mudarabah* contracts**, leaving microcredit-style debt instruments in their place, and the unbanked remain unbanked for cost and documentation reasons the mechanism does not address. *(continues in the scoring document)*

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.0.

**Mutual Credit / LETS: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/mc.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> **STRUCTURAL FAILURE.** Access breadth is, if anything, a genuine strength here — LETS and time banks are typically open to anyone in a community with essentially no entry barrier, unlike Market Socialism's membership-gated cooperative model — but C1.2a's analysis already establishes there is no asset-growth mechanism at all for that openness to apply to. Access to a mechanism that does not itself build assets does not satisfy this criterion's stated requirement, mirroring Georgism's identical C1.2a/C1.5 pairing immediately above.

**State Capitalism / Singapore (GLC Developmental Capitalism): 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/sg.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Among residents, access is close to universal: CPF membership and HDB eligibility reach nearly every citizen and PR household, and 91.2% of resident households own their home. Access is gated by pass type, however. HDB ownership is restricted to citizens and eligible PRs, foreigners pay 60% stamp duty on any home (Global Property Guide), and Work Permit holders — about a fifth of the population — have no pathway to long-term residency (HOME). Migrant-worker advocates report that first-time construction workers commonly pay S$13,000–16,000 in recruitment fees (HOME and TWC2, as reported by The Online Citizen, 2026), which makes early asset-building unlikely. This is the gated-access pattern of H.7v2's Market Socialism anchor. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**Status Quo Market Capitalism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/sq.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Only 60% have any wealth accumulation pathway at all — access, not just outcome, is structurally limited to those already positioned to reach capital ownership, falling well short of the 80% access-breadth threshold this narrowed criterion requires. *(Narrowed from the legacy version, which also carried a Gini clause now assigned solely to C1.2b above — see Revision Notice.)*

**Universal Basic Services: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/ubs.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> This narrowed criterion asks about breadth of *access* to an *active asset-growth mechanism* — not accumulation adequacy (C1.2a) or concentration prevention (C1.2b). Access breadth would, in principle, be a genuine strength of UBS: none of its seven sectors gate eligibility by employment status, membership, or means test in the stigmatizing sense (the entire universalist design premise is to avoid exactly that kind of gate). But C1.2a's analysis already establishes that there is no asset-growth mechanism at all for that openness to apply to — following the identical reasoning already established for Integral, UBI, Georgism, Mutual Credit/LETS, and Doughnut Economics, each of which fails this criterion for the same underlying design-level reason once C1.2a fails. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**MMT + Job Guarantee: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/mmt.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Universal employment provides universal income access, but this narrowed criterion asks specifically about access to an asset-*growth* mechanism — and the underlying vehicle is the same traditional-market one Status Quo Capitalism's own C1.5 reaches only ~60% of participants through, with guaranteed income improving the capacity to use it rather than adding a new one. Revised down from the legacy 1.0, which had conflated universal employment/income access with universal wealth-mechanism access.

**Fully Automated Luxury Communism: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/falc.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> **STRUCTURAL FAILURE.** Universal access to abundant goods and services is consumption access, not access to an asset-*growth* mechanism — following directly from C1.2a's own finding that no such mechanism is specified.

**Stakeholder Capitalism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/sc.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Slightly broader wealth access through expanded stock ownership programs. However, fundamental ownership concentration persists. The bottom 50% still hold minimal wealth despite stakeholder rhetoric.

**State Capitalism / China (Party-State-Directed Market Economy): 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/cn.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Measured by headcount, access looks broad (about 90% home ownership). Measured the way this criterion asks — an active pathway producing asset growth, not gated by membership or status — it is gated by hukou. Rural hukou holders' principal asset is a restricted use right (C1.2a); migrants face purchase restrictions in the largest cities; and the IMF (February 2026) describes hukou as effectively excluding rural migrants from full access to social benefits in large cities. That is the gated-access pattern H.7v2's Market Socialism anchor describes. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**State Capitalism / Qatar (Gulf Rentier-Distributive Statism): 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/qa.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> The narrowed threshold asks whether at least 80% of the population has an active wealth-accumulation pathway. Under the confirmed scope, the pathways described under C1.2a reach citizens and a minority of higher-paid foreign residents. The route into the citizen group is closed for practical purposes: *(continues in the scoring document)*

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5. Citizenship read as a membership gate

**Libertarian Minarchism: 0.5** (v1 0.0, up). [Evidence](evidence/lm.md#c1-5)

1. *≥80% population with an active wealth-accumulation pathway*: not shown. not shown: the design leaves wage saving, home and share ownership open to anyone through markets, gated by income rather than by prior wealth; no figure for the share with an active pathway is stated or located (the outside reader's closed-book estimate is 60-70%)

Read in the adjudication of the outside review (Session 83).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.0. The published reading: without public education and a safety net, effective access narrows to those born with capital (the 0.0 band)

**Doughnut Economics: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/de.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> This narrowed criterion asks about access *breadth* to an *active asset-growth mechanism* — not accumulation adequacy (C1.2a) or concentration prevention (C1.2b). Access breadth would, in principle, be one of Doughnut Economics' stronger claims: the framework's diagnostic and planning tools are explicitly designed to be participatory and open to a jurisdiction's entire population, with no membership gate or employment requirement of any kind. But C1.2a's analysis already establishes there is no asset-growth mechanism at all for that openness to apply to — following the identical reasoning already established for Georgism, Mutual Credit/LETS, and, originally, Integral's own C1.2/C1.5 pairing that first motivated the v2 split (Section 12.1). *(continues in the scoring document)*

**Centrally Planned Socialism: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/cps.md#c1-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> **STRUCTURAL FAILURE.** State ownership provides "universal" access in name only — since no individual wealth-accumulation mechanism exists at all (C1.2a, above), there is nothing for that access to be broad or narrow *to*. This is a downward revision from the legacy 0.5, which credited nominal universality without checking for an underlying mechanism to be universally accessing.

## Domain 2: Human Autonomy

Six criteria: freedom from coercion (C2.1), freedom from the need to work (C2.2), creative development (C2.3), democratic
participation (C2.4), exit (C2.5), and, new in v2.0, civil liberties and the rule of law (C2.6). v1 scored 37 of the domain's
115 scored cells at 1.0; v2.0 scores four of 138. Three of C2.1 to C2.4's thresholds are class I (v2.0 names the indicator),
and on the named indicators no entry clears every clause.

**C2.1, freedom from coercion.** None of the eight v1 passes holds. The second clause asks that self-reported autonomy be
validated by revealed preference, people accepting work or living arrangements they had refused once an alternative
exists, and no study or record shows it for any entry. Nordic Social Democracy clears the first clause on the survey's
latest year (92.0% to 94.4% satisfied with their freedom to choose, 2025); the designs clear it on their own texts. Five
entries fail: Centrally Planned Socialism, Libertarian Minarchism, Doughnut Economics, China (a state labour-transfer
programme for minority workers under surveillance) and Qatar. China's first clause (67.6%) turns on whether the latest year
or the three-year average is read, which the measurement field leaves open (ledger I12; the limits page).

**C2.2, freedom from the need to work.** A class W criterion: the pass held 20 cells. One pass, Fully Automated Luxury
Communism (held). Universal Basic Income's 1.0 falls in the adjudication (the literature includes partial and full
variants, and the threshold asks for 100% of basic needs for all residents), and CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP's falls by the owner's
decision 84.2 (it pays citizens and legal residents 18 and over, and the design's own model leaves adults below their cost
of living); each keeps a close call toward 1.0. Eleven entries fail, among them the four configured economies whose floors
are means-tested or categorical (Status Quo, China, Singapore, Qatar), read as no unconditional baseline. Whether a
conditional floor is the 0.5 band's "conditional" provision is a class question the limits page records (I12); Nordic
Social Democracy's generous conditional benefits are held at 0.5.

**C2.3, creative development.** None of the eight v1 passes holds; 22 entries score 0.5 and one 0.0. The pattern is one
clause of three: leisure hours (the second clause) are cleared from time-use data, while the share regularly engaged in
creative work and the meaning-and-purpose score are seldom measured. Nordic Social Democracy clears two (38 to 41 hours of
leisure a week; meaning-of-life ratings of 78 to 83 in EU-SILC's one wave) but not the first, since EU-SILC's 22% to 38%
practising artistic activities omits study and invention. Centrally Planned Socialism fails on the state's control of
cultural production (socialist realism the obligatory method; prior censorship from 1922 to 1991).

**C2.4, democratic participation.** None of the eight v1 passes holds. The threshold asks for 70% participation, 35% of
citizen proposals adopted, and 65% satisfied with responsiveness. Nordic Social Democracy is short on all three
(Finland's turnout 68.9% in 2023; at most 11% of Finland's citizens' initiatives adopted, and Sweden has no national
process; external efficacy of 21% to 44% in the OECD Trust Survey). The designs clear participation on their own texts and
state no adoption rate or satisfaction. A mechanism such as the Job Guarantee is out of reach on all three (0.5, not 0.0,
under the reach rule). Four fail: Centrally Planned Socialism, Fully Automated Luxury Communism, China and Qatar.

**C2.5, exit.** The domain's most mixed movement: v2.0 deleted clauses (class D), and every unit is read on the three that
remain. Three entries pass: Universal Basic Income (up from 0.0: an unconditional, portable payment makes
leaving a job or household feasible at once), Libertarian Minarchism (no exit cost, no compulsory participation, free
movement), and CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP on the owner's decision 79.2 (its close call toward 0.5 is the blind reading that an
early leaver forfeits most of Acre Equity). Nordic Social Democracy, Market Socialism, Degrowth and Fully Automated Luxury
Communism rise from 0.0 to 0.5. Eight v1 passes fall, each on a clause short, not shown or out of reach. Centrally Planned
Socialism falls to 0.0: emigration and non-participation were criminal offences under its own law.

**C2.6, civil liberties and the rule of law (new).** No entry passes; China (11 of 60 on Freedom House's civil-liberties
checklist) and Centrally Planned Socialism (the USSR in the rating's two lowest bands through the planned years) fail. Nordic
Social Democracy clears the first two clauses (57 to 60 of 60; informed consent in law) and is short on the third, because
Sweden's quarantine and area-closure provisions state no time limit. The United States is short on the first (49 of 60 in
2025). The designs are mostly silent on medical consent and compulsion; Libertarian Minarchism's platform states the
strongest guarantees of the 17 designs and mechanisms, with its first clause estimated at the bar (a close call toward
1.0). Six mechanisms are out of reach on every clause, and two more on some: a mechanism does not govern its host economy's
liberties, and out of reach scores 0.5.

**What the domain does not show.** C2.1, C2.3 and C2.4 rest on surveys that cover the configured economies unevenly (the
OECD Trust Survey does not cover China, and its coverage of Singapore, Qatar and the United States is unconfirmed), and
the designs are read on their own texts; the limits page lists the measures no source supplies.

<!-- generated:D2 -->
### C2.1 Freedom from Coercion

Pass Threshold (v2.0): ≥70% report genuine autonomy in major life decisions, validated through revealed preference showing acceptance of initially refused employment/living situations when alternatives exist. Revision class I (indicator named for an unchanged clause and bar).

v2.0: 0 at 1.0, 18 at 0.5, 5 at 0.0. v1: 8 at 1.0, 10 at 0.5, 5 at 0.0.

**CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/cco.md#c2-1)

1. *≥70% report genuine autonomy in major life decisions*: cleared. Modeling shows 75%+ report genuine autonomy
2. *validated through revealed preference showing acceptance of initially refused employment/living situations when alternatives exist*: not shown. no validation of the design's self-reported share by observed behaviour is stated or cited; the closest component evidence, OpenResearch's randomised study of $1,000 a month for three years to 1,000 low-income adults (control group $50 a month), found recipients' unemployment spells about a month longer with fewer applications, job seekers 5.5 points more likely to require interesting or meaningful work, and more moves of home and neighbourhood, which is behaviour of the kind the Paper's method names, but it reports no self-reported autonomy share and no correlation between self-report and behaviour (reading 3.3)

Read in part (b4) (Session 41).

**Nordic Social Democracy: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/nsd.md#c2-1)

2. *validated through revealed preference showing acceptance of initially refused employment/living situations when alternatives exist*: not shown. not shown: no study or record is found that shows acceptance of initially refused employment or living situations when an unconditional alternative exists, for this configuration (reading 2.1.2); Finland's 2017-2018 basic income experiment tested an unconditional payment outside the configuration's own institutions

### C2.2 Labor Non-Necessity

Pass Threshold (v2.0): Unconditional provision covering 100% of basic needs (housing, food, healthcare, utilities) for all residents. Revision class W (wording, commentary or disclosure only).

v2.0: 1 at 1.0, 11 at 0.5, 11 at 0.0. v1: 3 at 1.0, 9 at 0.5, 11 at 0.0.

**CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/cco.md#c2-2)

1. *Unconditional provision covering 100% of basic needs (housing, food, healthcare, utilities) for all residents*: not shown. not shown in full: the provision is unconditional (no means test or work requirement), but the design's legislative text pays 'every citizen and legal resident 18 years or older', its BLEI accounting values the Basic Units against food and utilities, essentials are paid partly in primary currency, and its published model leaves adults below their cost of living (the share falls 60.3% to 70.7% against no programme, the entry's C1.1)

Read in Session 84, then lowered to the adjudication's reading by the owner's decision 84.2 (ledger D67, Session 84).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 1.0. The published reading: the basic amount covers essential needs unconditionally

**Nordic Social Democracy: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/nsd.md#c2-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Generous unemployment and disability benefits provide baseline security, but still time-limited and conditional. Not truly unconditional; expectation of labor market participation remains.

**Market Socialism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/ms.md#c2-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Reduced coercion within employment, but survival still requires membership in a cooperative. Better than wage labor but not unconditional baseline security.

**Participatory Economics: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/pe.md#c2-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Effort-based remuneration still requires contribution. However, balanced job complexes and reduced hours make participation less burdensome. Not fully unconditional but substantial freedom compared to markets.

**Integral: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/int.md#c2-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> The philosophical commitment is strong — "human creativity and ingenuity have always driven activity... it is about giving people real freedom" — but the document is genuinely ambiguous about whether essentials are provided unconditionally or require some ITC contribution, stating only that "unnecessary labor is reduced" (not eliminated) alongside continued contribution tracking. NEEC requires unconditional baseline security without work requirements; Integral doesn't clearly provide this, even if culturally reframed and reduced.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.0 or 1.0. Essentials unconditional (1.0) or contribution-dependent (0.0)

**Sovereign Wealth Fund Statism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/swf.md#c2-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> NEEC's own Section 6 text already supplies this criterion's exact benchmark language for this system, again written before any system existed to claim it: the "Current Performance" line for C2.2 reads "Alaska PFD: Partial—universal but insufficient ($1,000–2,000 annually)." This is, like C1.1's Berman & Reamy citation, evidence the framework authors built in from the start, now finally scored under its native system rather than left as an unclaimed benchmark. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**Ostrom-Style Commons Governance: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/os.md#c2-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Principle 2's proportional equivalence between benefits and costs **ties appropriation to provision**: members earn their share by contributing to maintenance, monitoring and repair. That is a contribution requirement written into the design, which is the opposite of unconditional provision. Nor does any commons cover 100% of basic needs; it covers the goods that particular resource yields. *(continues in the scoring document)*

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5.

**Universal Basic Income: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/ubi.md#c2-2)

1. *Unconditional provision covering 100% of basic needs (housing, food, healthcare, utilities) for all residents*: not shown. not shown in full: the provision is unconditional, but the threshold asks that it cover 100% of basic needs (housing, food, healthcare, utilities) for all residents; the mechanism's literature includes partial and full variants (the definition sets no level), and the entry's own C1.3 records that rents can absorb the payment

Read in the adjudication of the outside review (Session 83).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 1.0. The 1.0 band's reading: provision explicitly unconditional

**Degrowth Economics: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/dg.md#c2-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Work-sharing and reduced hours move toward labor optionality but don't fully decouple survival from employment. Still assumes some labor participation for social contributions.

**Georgism / Land Value Tax (LVT + Citizen's Dividend): 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/geo.md#c2-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> *Dividend-dependent.* A Citizen's Dividend, structured as George proposed it ("share and share alike," unconditional, no means test), clears the unconditionality bar cleanly, which is a real strength distinct from means-tested welfare. What it does not clear at any evidenced real-world scale is covering 100% of basic needs — the transfer sizes located are a small fraction of a basic-needs budget in any high-income context, landing this between the unconditional-and-adequate Pass anchor and the no-baseline-at-all Fail anchor.

**Islamic Finance / Profit-Sharing Banking: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/if.md#c2-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> There is no baseline provision of any kind in the mechanism. H.7 notes this threshold is binary in character. One of the two failures in this evaluation that no flagged call disputes. Not flagged.

**Mutual Credit / LETS: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/mc.md#c2-2)

1. *Unconditional provision covering 100% of basic needs (housing, food, healthcare, utilities) for all residents*: short. short: credit can be extended before a member has contributed, but balances are capped and are repaid by supplying goods or services, so the system expects reciprocity over time rather than providing a baseline with no expectation of return

Read in the adjudication of the outside review (Session 83).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.0. The outside reader's reading: a capped, repayable credit line is no baseline independent of work

**State Capitalism / Singapore (GLC Developmental Capitalism): 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/sg.md#c2-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Support is deliberately tied to work or job search. Jobseeker Support requires job-search activity and ends when a job is found (MOM). ComCare short-term help lasts a median of six months, and about 49% of households that left it in 2021 returned within three years (MSF, via Malay Mail). The Workfare Income Supplement pays only those in work, and the Silver Support Scheme targets seniors with low lifetime incomes (MOF, via Mothership). In 2012 the then head of the national union federation, later Manpower Minister, said a minimum wage was not something Singapore embraced (People's World). *(continues in the scoring document)*

**Status Quo Market Capitalism: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/sq.md#c2-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> **STRUCTURAL FAILURE.** Survival entirely contingent on labor market participation or charity. No unconditional baseline security exists; unemployment benefits are temporary and conditional.

**Universal Basic Services: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/ubs.md#c2-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> NEEC's own threshold asks for "unconditional provision covering 100% of basic needs (housing, food, healthcare, utilities) for all residents" — language that maps almost directly onto UBS's own named sectors. Several UBS sectors genuinely clear an unconditional-access bar for the whole population (universal healthcare and education already function this way in the UK; free transport and basic information access are proposed as flatly universal, with no means test). But the shelter and food sectors, as actually costed, are targeted rather than flatly universal in the strongest sense: the housing plank provides zero-rent housing only to households allocated it on a needs basis (roughly 1.5 million of the UK's households, not all of them), and the food plank covers only a portion of meals for a defined food-insecure population (2.2 million households), not free food for every resident regardless of need. This is an important, and easy to miss, distinction: UBS's "universal" branding means "universally *available without stigma or means-testing bureaucracy* to anyone who needs it," not "everyone receives the same thing whether they need it or not" the way Universal Basic Income or CCO-PTF's baseline does. This is a real, substantive design difference from a clean unconditional-baseline system. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**MMT + Job Guarantee: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/mmt.md#c2-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> **STRUCTURAL FAILURE.** Ideologically committed to full employment. Treats joblessness as a problem to solve rather than potential liberation. Fundamentally rejects the premise that labor can become optional.

**Fully Automated Luxury Communism: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/falc.md#c2-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Defining features—labor becomes an optional hobby rather than survival requirement. Explicitly embodies labor non-necessity principle. Work as self-actualization, not compulsion.

**Stakeholder Capitalism: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/sc.md#c2-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> **STRUCTURAL FAILURE.** Does not address labor-income linkage. Assumes continued full employment. Fundamentally conservative reform maintaining the existing system with gentler management.

**State Capitalism / China (Party-State-Directed Market Economy): 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/cn.md#c2-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> The system's leadership rejects this criterion's premise explicitly. Xi Jinping's August 2021 speech on common prosperity, published in *Qiushi*, warns against setting excessively high goals or providing excessive guarantees, and against falling into a "welfarism" trap that encourages laziness (Caixin full text). His December 2021 remarks to the Central Economic Work Conference repeat the point, citing Latin American welfare states as the cautionary example (Ginger River translation). In practice, the only floor that resembles unconditional provision, dibao, is means-tested and shrinking — under 3% coverage in 2022 (C1.1). Pension eligibility is also tightening: the minimum contribution period rises from 15 to 20 years starting in 2030 (State Council Information Office). *(continues in the scoring document)*

**State Capitalism / Qatar (Gulf Rentier-Distributive Statism): 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/qa.md#c2-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Non-citizens have no baseline independent of work. Their residence depends on employment, and they are excluded from social assistance and the pension system (US Social Security Administration; HLB). Citizens have more: free land and housing finance (C1.3), free health care and state schooling, which the 2018 permanent-residency law extended to permanent residents (Al Jazeera), and an income-tested minimum income for specified groups (US Social Security Administration). But the citizen baseline is conditional: assistance is means-tested and limited to defined categories, and the main income channel is public employment. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**Libertarian Minarchism: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/lm.md#c2-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> **STRUCTURAL FAILURE.** Survival entirely contingent on market participation. No safety net whatsoever. Ideologically opposed to decoupling labor from survival.

**Doughnut Economics: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/de.md#c2-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> No unconditional baseline security of any kind — covering essentials without work requirements, behavior conditions, or means testing — exists anywhere in the Doughnut Economics framework or its implementations. This follows directly from the absence of any provision mechanism established in Domain 1. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**Centrally Planned Socialism: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/cps.md#c2-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> **STRUCTURAL FAILURE.** Despite guaranteed employment, labor was compulsory. "Work or prison" replaced "work or starve." Parasitism laws criminalized unemployment, creating different but equally coercive systems.

### C2.3 Creative Development Opportunities

### C2.4 Democratic Participation

Pass Threshold (v2.0): ≥70% participation in democratic processes, ≥35% citizen proposals adopted, ≥65% satisfaction with responsiveness. Revision class I (indicator named for an unchanged clause and bar).

v2.0: 0 at 1.0, 19 at 0.5, 4 at 0.0. v1: 8 at 1.0, 11 at 0.5, 4 at 0.0.

**CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/cco.md#c2-4)

1. *≥70% participation in democratic processes*: cleared. as audited; the design's documents give 15-25% as the portal's pilot success metric and model participation rising from 55% to 85% at maturity
2. *≥35% citizen proposals adopted*: not shown. any citizen may submit proposals above a signature threshold, and binding changes need a 60-67% supermajority with a geographic distribution requirement; no estimate of the share adopted (reading 3.5)
3. *≥65% satisfaction with responsiveness*: not shown. satisfaction with democratic processes is named as a metric, with no estimate

Read in part (b1) (Session 38).

**Nordic Social Democracy: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/nsd.md#c2-4)

1. *≥70% participation in democratic processes*: short. short: voting-age turnout at the latest parliamentary election is DNK 73.3% (2026), FIN 68.9% (2023), NOR 73.1% (2025), SWE 80.3% (2026): Finland is below 70% (reading 2.4.1)
2. *≥35% citizen proposals adopted*: short. short: Finland's citizens' initiative had brought 64 proposals to Parliament by March 2023, of which five became law and two more in modified form (at most 11%); Sweden has no national process (reading 2.4.2)
3. *≥65% satisfaction with responsiveness*: short. short: the OECD Trust Survey's external-efficacy share (2023) is DNK 44.3%, FIN 20.9%, NOR 34.8%, SWE 29.9%, each below 65% (reading 2.4.3)

### C2.5 Exit Rights and Mobility

Pass Threshold (v2.0): Exit feasible within 3 months without material penalty, no differential treatment, geographic mobility maintained. Revision class D (clauses deleted only).

v2.0: 3 at 1.0, 19 at 0.5, 1 at 0.0. v1: 10 at 1.0, 8 at 0.5, 5 at 0.0.

**CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/cco.md#c2-5)

1. *Exit feasible within 3 months without material penalty*: cleared. on the owner's reading (decision 79.2), exit is leaving the system entirely: a member may stop taking Basic Units at any time, with no work requirement or other condition attached; Public Trust Housing's tenure-graded access to Acre Equity at exit (about 10-20% in the first six months, rising to 80-90% after five years) is read not as a material penalty but as a provision for the stability of the remaining members, on an asset a non-member never accrues: a member who leaves early keeps part of an equity that a renter outside the system does not build, so is no worse off than one who never joined; a member who moves (a job transfer, a new home) moves within the network keeping the equity
2. *no differential treatment*: cleared. cleared: participation is opt-in only, the design preserves private markets and ownership, and its validation tracks non-participants' outcomes so that they are not harmed (reading 2.5.2)
3. *geographic mobility maintained*: cleared. cleared: members may move between Public Trust Housing properties keeping their equity status ('network portability'), and the Basic Unit allocation is made to citizens who opt in, not to a place (reading 2.5.3)

Read in part (b5) (Session 42) and re-read blind in Session 75, then raised by the owner's decision 79.2 (Session 79).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5. The blind re-read's reading (Session 75, reading 2.5.1): Acre Equity is accessible at exit only in part in the early years, so an early leaver forfeits most of it, a material penalty

**Nordic Social Democracy: 0.5** (v1 0.0, up). [Evidence](evidence/nsd.md#c2-5)

1. *Exit feasible within 3 months without material penalty*: cleared. cleared in each of the four: emigration is free; exit taxes reach unrealised share gains only and are deferred within the EEA; accrued pensions are paid abroad (reading 2.5.1)
2. *no differential treatment*: short. short: social assistance and unemployment benefit are conditioned on availability for work in each of the four (Denmark's 2024 work obligation among them) (reading 2.5.2)
3. *geographic mobility maintained*: cleared. cleared: national benefits are portable within each economy, and EEA coordination and the Nordic Convention carry entitlements across borders (reading 2.5.3)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 75.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.0. 0.5: free exit and portable benefits, with activation conditions on assistance; residents cannot opt out of the tax base short of emigrating

**Market Socialism: 0.5** (v1 0.0, up). [Evidence](evidence/ms.md#c2-5)

1. *Exit feasible within 3 months without material penalty*: cleared. cleared: workers may leave a self-managed firm at once, holding no capital stake to forfeit (Schweickart); the citizen's coupon portfolio is a dividend right, not a sold stake (Roemer) (reading 2.5.1)
2. *no differential treatment*: short. short: a private firm may operate only at small scale and passes to its workers when its owner sells or retires (Schweickart), so those who stay outside worker self-management are treated differently (reading 2.5.2)
3. *geographic mobility maintained*: not shown. not shown: the sources do not address relocation or the portability of benefits (reading 2.5.3)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 75.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.0. 0.5: free exit from firms within a socialised economy that bars large private capital

**Participatory Economics: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/pe.md#c2-5)

1. *Exit feasible within 3 months without material penalty*: short. short: with private productive property and markets abolished, there is no arrangement to leave for within the economy (reading 2.5.1)
2. *no differential treatment*: cleared. cleared: besides the effort-rated budget, the design allows 'a consumption allowance for people who simply don't want to work' at a level meeting basic needs (Wright's statement of the procedure in Hahnel and Wright, 2016, kept by Hahnel; Session 71's record, section 6), so non-participants are not penalised (reading 2.5.2)
3. *geographic mobility maintained*: not shown. not shown: the sources do not address relocation or the portability of benefits (reading 2.5.3)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 75.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.0. 0.5: membership of councils is the economy; no alternative arrangement exists within it

**Integral: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/int.md#c2-5)

1. *Exit feasible within 3 months without material penalty*: cleared. cleared: participation 'remains voluntary' throughout the transition and Integral coexists with market production; a leaver loses only unused time credits, which are non-transferable access signals that decay (reading 2.5.1)
2. *no differential treatment*: not shown. not shown: essentials stay accessible 'regardless of age or ability', but access is otherwise earned by contribution, and the paper does not say how non-participants fare once Integral displaces market production (reading 2.5.2)
3. *geographic mobility maintained*: cleared. cleared: a member who relocates has their account migrated to the new node by an equivalence rule (reading 2.5.3)

Read in part (b5) (Session 42) and re-read blind in Session 75.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 1.0. 0.5: voluntary, federated and portable; the treatment of non-participants after displacement is unstated

**Sovereign Wealth Fund Statism: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/swf.md#c2-5)

1. *Exit feasible within 3 months without material penalty*: out of reach. out of reach: a state fund is not an arrangement one enters or leaves, and the dividend does not fund leaving other arrangements (reading 2.5.1; D28(c))
2. *no differential treatment*: short. short: the dividend requires a full calendar year's residency and excludes those incarcerated on a felony conviction (Alaska Statutes 43.23.005), so new and some residents are treated differently (reading 2.5.2)
3. *geographic mobility maintained*: cleared. cleared: generalised to a national economy, the dividend is kept on moving within it (reading 2.5.3)

Read in part (b5) (Session 42) and re-read blind in Session 75.

**Ostrom-Style Commons Governance: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/os.md#c2-5)

1. *Exit feasible within 3 months without material penalty*: cleared. cleared: members may leave a commons institution at any time, losing only the use right (reading 2.5.1)
2. *no differential treatment*: cleared. cleared: non-members are excluded from the resource but not otherwise penalised (reading 2.5.2)
3. *geographic mobility maintained*: short. short: use rights are held by members of a bounded local community (design principle 1; the Törbel commons), so they are lost on relocating (reading 2.5.3)

Read in part (b5) (Session 42) and re-read blind in Session 75.

**Universal Basic Income: 1.0** (v1 0.0, up). [Evidence](evidence/ubi.md#c2-5)

1. *Exit feasible within 3 months without material penalty*: cleared. cleared: an unconditional individual payment makes leaving a job, household or community feasible at once without losing income -- the measurement's capacity to leave for alternative arrangements (reading 2.5.1)
2. *no differential treatment*: cleared. cleared: paid to every member without means test or work requirement (Van Parijs and Vanderborght) (reading 2.5.2)
3. *geographic mobility maintained*: cleared. cleared: national and individual, so it moves with the recipient within the economy (reading 2.5.3)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 75.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.0. 1.0: the paradigm exit-enabling mechanism: no participation, no condition, portable

**Degrowth Economics: 0.5** (v1 0.0, up). [Evidence](evidence/dg.md#c2-5)

1. *Exit feasible within 3 months without material penalty*: not shown. not shown: the sources state no terms for leaving the system (reading 2.5.1)
2. *no differential treatment*: not shown. not shown: caps on resource use and income bind every resident alike; the sources say nothing of treatment of those who stay outside degrowth's institutions (reading 2.5.2)
3. *geographic mobility maintained*: not shown. not shown: the sources do not address relocation or the portability of benefits (reading 2.5.3)

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.0. 0.5: no exit terms stated; its caps are laws binding all, not a participation one could leave

**Georgism / Land Value Tax (LVT + Citizen's Dividend): 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/geo.md#c2-5)

1. *Exit feasible within 3 months without material penalty*: out of reach. out of reach: a land value tax is not an arrangement one enters or leaves, and the dividend is too small to fund leaving other arrangements (reading 2.5.1; D28(c))
2. *no differential treatment*: cleared. cleared: the tax falls on land whoever holds it and the citizen's dividend is paid to all alike (reading 2.5.2)
3. *geographic mobility maintained*: cleared. cleared: the dividend is national, and replacing transaction taxes lowers the cost of moving (reading 2.5.3)

**Islamic Finance / Profit-Sharing Banking: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/if.md#c2-5)

1. *Exit feasible within 3 months without material penalty*: cleared. cleared: either partner may end a musharaka or mudaraba by notice, and diminishing-musharaka home finance is settled by buying the remaining units (reading 2.5.1)
2. *no differential treatment*: cleared. cleared: the contracts are open to all on the same terms (reading 2.5.2)
3. *geographic mobility maintained*: out of reach. out of reach: financing contracts govern no benefit a move could lose (reading 2.5.3; D28(c))

1. *Exit feasible within 3 months without material penalty*: short. short: balances are in a unit not convertible to national currency, so a member leaving with a credit balance forfeits it, and one in debit must settle (reading 2.5.1)
2. *no differential treatment*: cleared. cleared: non-members are not penalised (reading 2.5.2)
3. *geographic mobility maintained*: short. short: networks are local, so a member who relocates loses the network and its balance (reading 2.5.3)

**State Capitalism / Singapore (GLC Developmental Capitalism): 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/sg.md#c2-5)

1. *Exit feasible within 3 months without material penalty*: short. short: national-service-liable males need exit permits and may not renounce citizenship before completing national service; otherwise CPF balances are released on renouncing and leaving (reading 2.5.1)

### C2.6 Civil Liberties and Rule of Law

Pass Threshold (v2.0): Civil-liberties score ≥53 of 60 on the Freedom House checklist; informed consent to medical treatment protected in law; any compulsory treatment, vaccination, quarantine or isolation prescribed by law, time-limited and open to independent judicial review. Revision class N (new criterion).

v2.0: 0 at 1.0, 21 at 0.5, 2 at 0.0. New in v2.0.

**CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP: 0.5** (new in v2.0). [Evidence](evidence/cco.md#c2-6)

1. *Civil-liberties score ≥53 of 60 on the Freedom House checklist*: not shown. not shown, estimate 46 to 54: the PTH charter states freedom of expression, assembly and political activity, privacy against surveillance, fair hearings before penalties, equal protection, 'notice, hearing, and appeal rights' for all enforcement, and that community governance shall not infringe constitutionally protected rights; independent courts hear discrimination complaints and zone decisions get independent review; personal data is held under zero-knowledge proofs and user-held keys. Against them: the risk framework's automated transaction limits and account restrictions reach judicial review only at the 'critical' level, merit assessments are appealed to 'democratic review', the 'independent judiciary' of PTH communities is an elected grievance committee, and officials' 'factual honesty' is enforceable by Judicial Guard referral and prosecution, which bears on expression (D1, D4) and due process (F2) (reading 2.6.1)
2. *informed consent to medical treatment protected in law*: not shown. not shown: the sources are silent on consent to medical treatment (reading 2.6.3)
3. *any compulsory treatment, vaccination, quarantine or isolation prescribed by law, time-limited and open to independent judicial review*: not shown. not shown: the sources are silent on compulsory treatment, vaccination, quarantine or isolation (reading 2.6.4)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 76.

**Nordic Social Democracy: 0.5** (new in v2.0). [Evidence](evidence/nsd.md#c2-6)

1. *Civil-liberties score ≥53 of 60 on the Freedom House checklist*: cleared. cleared: DNK 57, FIN 60, NOR 60, SWE 59 of 60 in 2025; V-Dem DNK 0.959, FIN 0.947, NOR 0.947, SWE 0.960, the cross-check agreeing (reading 2.6.1)
2. *informed consent to medical treatment protected in law*: cleared. cleared in each of the four: Denmark, 'no treatment may be begun or continued without the patient's informed consent', which may be withdrawn at any time (Health Act, section 15); Finland, the patient is treated 'in mutual understanding' and a refused treatment is replaced by another acceptable one, care against the patient's will only under the mental-health, substance-abuse, communicable-diseases and special-care acts (Patients' Rights Act, section 6); Sweden, 'care may not be given without the patient's consent', which may be withdrawn at any time (Patient Act, chapter 4, section 2); Norway, 'health care may only be given with the patient's consent', revocable, exceptions only in the communicable-diseases, mental-health and care acts (Patient and User Rights Act, section 4-1) (reading 2.6.2)
3. *any compulsory treatment, vaccination, quarantine or isolation prescribed by law, time-limited and open to independent judicial review*: short. short in two of the four, so for the configuration: in Sweden a quarantine (Communicable Diseases Act, chapter 3, section 9) and an area closure (section 10) state no time limit, being lifted only when the grounds lapse, and the Public Health Agency's closure cannot be appealed except as a refusal of exemption (chapter 8, section 1); in Finland a standing requirement bars health and social-care workers without measles, varicella and influenza vaccination from work with vulnerable patients except for a special reason (Communicable Diseases Act, section 48), a penalty of refusal by loss of work with no lapse. Denmark's Epidemics Act meets the clause (admission, isolation and treatment orders of at most four weeks, renewable, each brought before a court within five weekdays; general rules valid four weeks at a time), as do Finland's quarantine (at most a month) and isolation (two months) with appeal to the administrative court and Sweden's isolation by the administrative court (three months, then six at a time); Norway's acts were not reached (reading 2.6.3)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 77.

## Domain 3: System Resilience

Six criteria: crisis response (C3.1), inflation control (C3.2), resistance to compound failures (C3.3), adaptability (C3.4),
transparency about failure (C3.5), and, new in v2.0, productive and innovative capacity (C3.6). v1 scored 40 of the 115 cells
it scored at 1.0; v2.0 scores ten of 138, three of them on the new criterion. Resilience is the domain where a design's
description of a mechanism most often stood in for a record of it working, and the domain where v2.0's thresholds ask most
often for a record.

**C3.1, crisis response.** None of the seven v1 passes holds. The designs clear the first clause (a response within 72 hours)
on their automatic stabilisers and fall on the second, support that rises one for one with the severity of the crisis:
Nordic Social Democracy's support rises by widening coverage at fixed replacement rates, not per person. Status Quo rises
from 0.0 to 0.5: in 2020 social benefits to households rose 35.5% while real GDP fell 2.1%, which clears the second clause,
but the first official data showing the shock came on 26 March and 78.8% of the population is covered by a cash benefit.
Three fail: Libertarian Minarchism; Doughnut Economics, which adopts no stabilisation instrument (down from 0.5); and Qatar,
where 4.1% of residents are covered by a cash benefit and the migrant majority by none (down from 0.5).

**C3.2, inflation control.** Two pass. CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP holds its 1.0 on the owner's decision 58.1, which reads the
design's backing of the currency by creative output as backing what it pays; its second clause (inflation of 5% or less when
outside inflation is 8%) is carried on the framework paper's 8% scenario (local inflation 3.2% against national 8.1%), since
the design's published model runs no 8% scenario. China's v1 1.0 is held at the published score, with a close call toward
0.5. Seven v1 passes fall. The decisive record is 2022: every Nordic economy ran inflation above 5% in every month from May
to October (Denmark 11.4% in October), so Nordic Social Democracy is short on the stress clause. Centrally Planned Socialism
falls because official prices held by decree hid inflation in queues and rationing rather than preventing it.

**C3.3, compound failures.** A class W criterion; no entry passes. The four v1 passes fall: Degrowth's, Participatory
Economics' and Integral's in the adjudication, since none models the four compound scenarios, and CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP's on its
own model, whose one compound scenario (the Adverse environment) is milder than the criterion's. Status Quo rises from 0.0
to 0.5 in the adjudication: on the criterion's own poverty measure the 2008 to 2009 shock did not degrade the function.
Libertarian Minarchism and Centrally Planned Socialism are held at 0.0.

**C3.4, adaptability.** Five pass, each with a close call toward 0.5, and twelve of v1's seventeen passes fall. Market
Socialism passes on a record: Mondragon's pay-ratio cap moved from 1:3 at founding to 1:6 in 1988, a 100% range, decided by
the cooperatives' General Assemblies, with no collapse caused by an adjustment located. CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP passes on the
owner's decision 79.3, which reads clause 4 (zero collapses during parameter adjustments) as excluding a smaller
allowance's direct effect, with clauses 1 and 3 sourced in Session 84 (`NEEC_OwnerItems_s84.md`, section 3). Integral and
Ostrom-Style Commons Governance pass on the owner's decision 79.4 (clause 4 cleared where clauses 1 to 3 are cleared and no
collapse is located); Sovereign Wealth Fund Statism passes on Norway's record (the fund's equity share moved from 40% to 70%
between 1998 and 2017 without collapse, the 2017 decisions eleven weeks after the central bank's advice) and Alaska's
(the 2016 dividend veto challenged in the state Supreme Court). Degrowth and Participatory Economics, raised by 79.4, return
to 0.5 by the owner's decision 84.1: clauses that 79.4's rule needs cleared were not shown on sources.
Nordic Social Democracy is short on the fourth: the financial liberalisation of the 1980s led to systemic banking crises in
Finland, Norway and Sweden. Fully Automated Luxury Communism falls to 0.0 (no procedure for adjusting its rules is specified)
and Libertarian Minarchism rises from 0.0 to 0.5.

**C3.5, transparency about failure.** No entry passes; the three v1 passes fall, Centrally Planned Socialism's to 0.0 on its
active suppression of failure information (the Kyshtym accident of 1957 acknowledged only in 1989; the famine of 1932 to 1933
denied). China fails on the same ground (the epidemic warning punished as false information; a State Council investigation
found deaths in the Henan floods withheld). Three mechanisms fail because a transfer or a guarantee has no channel that
detects or corrects failures (Universal Basic Income, Universal Basic Services, MMT + Job Guarantee). Four entries rise from
0.0 to 0.5, among them Nordic Social Democracy (implicit fossil-fuel subsidies under 1% of GDP; Sweden's audit office reports
action on nearly 85% of its recommendations within a year).

**C3.6, productive and innovative capacity (new).** Three pass, all configured economies, each with a close call toward 0.5:
Status Quo (output per hour up 1.30% a year and total factor productivity 0.45% a year over 20 years), China (8.18% and
3.02%) and Singapore (2.31% and 0.36%). Nordic Social Democracy is short on total factor productivity, which fell in Finland and Norway over the
20 years. No design shows the clauses: their productivity figures are projections, and none accounts for basic-needs output
at its own working time; the mechanisms are out of reach. So the criterion separates the measured economies from the
designs by evidence, not by a finding that the designs would be less productive.

**What the domain does not show.** The designs' resilience is read on their own models and texts; none has an
implementation that has met a compound shock. The limits page states it, with the clause readings that rest on a design's
own text.

### C3.2 Inflation Control Mechanisms

Pass Threshold (v2.0): Long-term inflation ≤3%, stress test inflation ≤5% (when external 8%), automatic adjustment preventing runaway inflation. Revision class U (unchanged).

v2.0: 2 at 1.0, 21 at 0.5, 0 at 0.0. v1: 9 at 1.0, 14 at 0.5, 0 at 0.0.

**CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/cco.md#c3-2)

1. *Long-term inflation ≤3%*: cleared. on the owner's reading the design's backing of the currency by creative output, a community chest, is an original feature of the design and is read as backing what the Source pays; at full backing the design's published model raises prices 0.1% a year over 20 years in the Reference environment, in every seed under 3%; the dual-currency paper's own simulations give 0.95% a year; held until an empirical study shows that the backing does not hold in real-world data
2. *stress test inflation ≤5% (when external 8%)*: cleared. carried, as group 2.1 carried it, on the framework paper's 8% scenario, in which local inflation is held to 3.2% while national inflation reaches 8.1%; the design's published model carries no 8% scenario, so it does not contradict the figure
3. *automatic adjustment preventing runaway inflation*: cleared. carried, as group 2.1 carried it, on the design's automatic parameter adjustment (the dual-currency paper's adjustment rules); with every dollar the Source pays backed by new output, the model shows no runaway inflation for an adjustment to prevent

Read in decision 58.1 (Session 58).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5. The owner's decision 58.1 restores the published 1.0 and supersedes group 2.5's unit (decision 57.7, S57-3.9); it reopens toward 0.5 on an empirical study showing that the backing by creative output does not hold in real-world data

**Nordic Social Democracy: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/nsd.md#c3-2)

1. *Long-term inflation ≤3%*: cleared. Successfully maintained low inflation (2-3%) over decades
2. *stress test inflation ≤5% (when external 8%)*: short. in the same episode every Nordic economy ran HICP inflation above 5% in every month from May to October 2022; in October Denmark stood at 11.4%, Sweden 9.8%, Finland 8.4%, Norway 8.4% and Iceland 6.4%, against 10.6% in the euro area and 2.9% in Switzerland, which shows the 5% bar was attainable in that environment (reading 3.4)
3. *automatic adjustment preventing runaway inflation*: not shown. not estimated in this pass; the verdict is fixed by clause 2

Read in part (b4) (Session 41).

**Market Socialism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/ms.md#c3-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Market mechanisms similar to capitalism. No specific innovations in inflation control. Democratic wage-setting could moderate wage-price spirals but unproven at scale.

**Participatory Economics: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/pe.md#c3-2)

1. *Long-term inflation ≤3%*: cleared. prevents systemic price instability
2. *stress test inflation ≤5% (when external 8%)*: not shown. facilitation boards adjusting indicative prices through the iteration process are specified; no estimate of inflation under an external 8% scenario is stated or cited, and the clause applies because the design keeps indicative prices (reading 3.4)
3. *automatic adjustment preventing runaway inflation*: cleared. adjusting indicative prices to balance supply/demand

Read in part (b4) (Session 41).

**Integral: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/int.md#c3-2)

1. *Long-term inflation ≤3%*: cleared. there is no money supply to inflate
2. *stress test inflation ≤5% (when external 8%)*: not shown. not shown: the white paper does specify the boundary -- Interface Cooperatives let the design 'operate inside a monetary environment without internalizing monetary logic', 'a containment strategy: money is allowed to cross the boundary only where necessary, and only in one direction' (section 11.6) -- correcting the earlier reading's 'a mechanism the design does not specify'; but no source estimates the system under an external 8% inflation while its nodes buy from markets, and the paper calls the hybrid integration 'an experimental concept that needs more development'
3. *automatic adjustment preventing runaway inflation*: cleared. matched to capacity through COS

Read in part (b4) (Session 41), then adjudicated after the outside review (Session 83).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 1.0. The outside reader's reading: no internal prices and a one-way boundary keep internal inflation at zero, as clause 1 is read

**Sovereign Wealth Fund Statism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/swf.md#c3-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> A specific, purpose-built, well-documented design feature exists that most systems in this corpus cannot point to: GPFG invests exclusively abroad, a deliberate sterilization of the resource windfall from the domestic economy that is the textbook response to the Dutch- disease/currency-appreciation risk a windfall of this size would otherwise create. This is more distinctive than a generic "inherits whatever monetary policy the host country has" 0.5, but it addresses one specific inflation channel (resource-windfall sterilization) rather than the sectoral-demand-isolation and velocity-control apparatus the Pass Threshold specifies, and no evidence was located of either exemplar being stress-tested against an 8%-external-inflation scenario. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**Ostrom-Style Commons Governance: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/os.md#c3-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> The mechanism has **no monetary function of any kind**: no unit of account, no credit creation, no reserve, no price-level instrument, no fiscal channel. The reason is simple absence rather than malfunction. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**Universal Basic Income: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/ubi.md#c3-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Major vulnerability. Most proposals lack specific inflation control beyond hoping taxation removes equivalent currency. Sectoral inflation risk (housing, healthcare) unaddressed. Requires complementary policies (price controls, supply enhancement).

**Degrowth Economics: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/dg.md#c3-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Local currencies, time banks, and reduced monetization could limit monetary inflation. However, a transition period from growth economy to degrowth risks stagflation. Unproven inflation management.

**Georgism / Land Value Tax (LVT + Citizen's Dividend): 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/geo.md#c3-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> No specific inflation-control innovation is part of Georgist theory; a Georgist economy inherits whatever monetary policy its host country runs, the same reasoning already applied to Market Socialism and Stakeholder Capitalism elsewhere in this Report.

**Islamic Finance / Profit-Sharing Banking: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/if.md#c3-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> The mechanism supplies no distinct inflation-control instrument; outcomes track the host monetary framework. IMF Working Paper 16/72 found that low and stable inflation and a working transmission mechanism are attainable with a significant Islamic banking segment — Brunei, Bahrain, and Malaysia recorded the lowest inflation among such countries — while Iran, Sudan, Pakistan, and Yemen were identified as needing a stronger monetary framework. That contrast is informative precisely because it cuts across contract type: the two whole-system cases sit on the weak side, so the inflation record follows the monetary regime rather than the prohibition of interest. Malaysia's MYOR-i, mandated for Islamic products from the second half of 2027, is a benchmark rate, not a price-stability mechanism. H.7's 0.5 band — "relies on conventional monetary policy without novel safeguards" — applies. Not flagged.

**Mutual Credit / LETS: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/mc.md#c3-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> The zero-sum-by-construction design is a genuine structural anti-inflation property: credit is created only at the moment of a matched trade, with no mechanism internal to a well-run system for the money supply to expand faster than real trading activity. Argentina's Redes de Trueque supplies a dramatic counter-example: as the network scaled into the millions, internal "créditos" vouchers used by several sub-networks were extensively counterfeited, and issuing organizations reportedly printed excess vouchers to keep pace with demand, producing severe internal inflation and a collapse in trust identified as a central cause of the network's disintegration. The zero-sum property is a feature of a *well-governed*, well-verified system, not an unconditional guarantee.

**State Capitalism / Singapore (GLC Developmental Capitalism): 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/sg.md#c3-2)

1. *Long-term inflation ≤3%*: cleared. cleared: core inflation averaged 0.7% in 2025 and long-run inflation is within the bar
2. *stress test inflation ≤5% (when external 8%)*: short. short: headline inflation averaged 6.1% in 2022, the stress year, above the bar of 5% (core 4.1%), the measure on which the pass read Status Quo's clause 2 (part (b4))
3. *automatic adjustment preventing runaway inflation*: not shown. not estimated: whether the MAS's exchange-rate band, reset at its policy reviews, is automatic adjustment is left to Report v2.0, as for Status Quo

Read in the adjudication of the outside review (Session 83).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 1.0. The published reading: core inflation of 4.1% in 2022 below the stress bar

**Status Quo Market Capitalism: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/sq.md#c3-2)

1. *Long-term inflation ≤3%*: cleared. maintains long-term inflation around 2-3% target
2. *stress test inflation ≤5% (when external 8%)*: short. the 2021-23 shock is the documented stress episode, with global inflation averaging 8.7% in 2022: US consumer prices rose 7.0% over 2021, 8.5% in the year to March 2022 and 9.1% in the year to June 2022, the largest twelve-month rise since 1981; prices excluding food and energy rose 6.6% in the year to September 2022, so the bar is missed on the core measure as well (reading 3.4)
3. *automatic adjustment preventing runaway inflation*: not shown. not estimated in this pass; the verdict is fixed by clause 2 (whether rate-setting by the Federal Open Market Committee is automatic adjustment is left to Report v2.0)

Read in part (b4) (Session 41).

**Universal Basic Services: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/ubs.md#c3-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> UBS has no monetary-policy innovation of its own and inherits whatever the host country's central bank does — the same reasoning already applied to Market Socialism, Stakeholder Capitalism, Georgism, and Doughnut Economics. One narrow, real point worth crediting specifically: for the roughly one-fifth to one-third of a typical household budget UBS's covered sectors represent (housing, transport, and a share of food), market-price inflation in those specific categories no longer directly affects covered households' material welfare, since the price to the user is fixed at zero regardless of the state's own underlying procurement costs — a narrower, sector-specific version of the "sectoral demand isolation" logic credited more fully to CCO-PTF elsewhere in this corpus. This insulates household welfare in those categories without insulating the state's own fiscal exposure to inflation in them. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**MMT + Job Guarantee: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/mmt.md#c3-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Buffer stock employment theoretically anchors wages and prices. However, the mechanism is unproven at scale. Risk of wage-price spiral if job guarantee wage set too high. Requires careful calibration.

**Fully Automated Luxury Communism: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/falc.md#c3-2)

1. *Long-term inflation ≤3%*: cleared. post-scarcity abundance claimed to remove inflation (a design's projection)
2. *stress test inflation ≤5% (when external 8%)*: not shown. not shown
3. *automatic adjustment preventing runaway inflation*: short. assumes the problem away through technology: no mechanism of its own, which D26 places in the 0.5 band

Read in part (a) (Session 37).

**Stakeholder Capitalism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/sc.md#c3-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> No innovations beyond traditional monetary policy. ESG metrics don't address macroeconomic stability. Inherits market capitalism's inflation control mechanisms and limitations.

**State Capitalism / China (Party-State-Directed Market Economy): 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/cn.md#c3-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> China passed this threshold's stress test in the one recent real-world instance of it. Consumer prices rose 2.0% in 2022, below the government's 3% target, while inflation ran around 8% in the United States, above 8% in the eurozone, and around 9% in the UK (National Development and Reform Commission, via SCIO). The mechanisms are specific and named. In September 2022 the state released about 200,000 tons of pork from government reserves, its largest monthly release on record, and it runs price-management schemes intended to keep pork and coal prices within set ranges (gov.cn), alongside conventional monetary policy. Consumer inflation peaked at 2.8% that September (Statista), and long-run inflation has stayed within the threshold's 3% bar. *(continues in the scoring document)*

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5. Symmetric price-stability (deflation) reading

**State Capitalism / Qatar (Gulf Rentier-Distributive Statism): 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/qa.md#c3-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> The riyal is pegged to the US dollar, and the Qatar Central Bank sets its policy rates in close alignment with the Federal Reserve's (IMF, 2025). Consumer-price inflation averaged 3.2% a year from 1980 to 2024 and peaked at 15.1% in 2008, before turning negative in 2009 (IMF data, via TheGlobalEconomy.com); monthly inflation peaked at 16.59% in June 2008 (National Planning Council data, via Trading Economics). Recent inflation is low: 1.3% in 2024 (IMF) and 2.51% in February 2026 (Trading Economics). The long-run average sits just above the 3% threshold, and the 2008 peak was about three times the 5% stress bound (computed). Because a peg imports US monetary policy, it cannot tighten against a purely domestic boom (a structural observation, not a sourced finding about 2008). *(continues in the scoring document)*

**Libertarian Minarchism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/lm.md#c3-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Gold standard or commodity money could theoretically control inflation. However, it eliminates monetary policy flexibility needed for economic stabilization. Rigid money supply creates deflation risks.

**Doughnut Economics: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/de.md#c3-2)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Doughnut Economics has no monetary system, currency, or fiscal instrument of its own; it is layered onto whatever monetary policy the host jurisdiction already runs, exactly the same reasoning this paper already applies to Market Socialism, Stakeholder Capitalism, and Georgism. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**Centrally Planned Socialism: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/cps.md#c3-2)

1. *Long-term inflation ≤3%*: short. official prices held by decree while excess demand appeared as queues, rationing and black markets (the entry's shortage inflation): repressed and hidden inflation, not price stability
2. *stress test inflation ≤5% (when external 8%)*: not shown. no stress record separable from the system's end
3. *automatic adjustment preventing runaway inflation*: short. no automatic adjustment: the monetary overhang was released as open inflation when prices were freed at the system's end

Read in part (a) (Session 37).

### C3.3 Multi-Failure Resistance

Pass Threshold (v2.0): Maintain core functions across ≥3 of 4 compound scenarios with degradation <20%. Revision class W (wording, commentary or disclosure only).

v2.0: 0 at 1.0, 21 at 0.5, 2 at 0.0. v1: 4 at 1.0, 16 at 0.5, 3 at 0.0.

1. *Maintain core functions across ≥3 of 4 compound scenarios with degradation <20%*: not shown. the design's published model at v5.6.0 runs one compound scenario, the Adverse environment (recessions, an automation wave and 2% outside inflation at the reference settings), milder than C3.3's recession-and-automation scenario, and a weaker-settings variant of it, the Stress Test; against the Reference environment on the same seeds at year 20, the share of adults not below the cost of living degrades 28.6% (release row) and 40.0% (full backing), and the share not in housing distress 28.7% and 14.7%; the share above the Societal Poverty Line degrades under 1%; essential-service and democratic-function capacity are not modelled, and the other three compound scenarios are not modelled (reading 3.10)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Better than pure market systems due to stronger safety nets, but still vulnerable to compound crises. Small open economies exposed to global shocks; 2008-2012 showed vulnerability despite better resilience than others.

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

**Participatory Economics: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/pe.md#c3-3)

Read in the adjudication of the outside review (Session 83).
