**Sovereign Wealth Fund Statism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/swf.md#c3-3)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> GPFG's own diversification is extreme by any standard — roughly 1.5% of all globally listed equity, spread across more than 7,000 companies and dozens of countries — providing real, if incidental, insulation against any single-country or single-sector shock. But this is a property of the investment portfolio's construction, not a designed response to compound domestic shocks across economic, environmental, and social domains simultaneously, and no evidence was located of either exemplar being tested against NEEC's own four named compound scenarios. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**Ostrom-Style Commons Governance: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/os.md#c3-3)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> **The longevity evidence is the best in the corpus by an order of magnitude.** Valencia's water tribunal has carried its core function through conquest, dynastic change, war and dictatorship for roughly a millennium; Törbel's alpine commons date from the thirteenth century; Araral's *zanjera* case is four hundred years old; Japanese irrigation institutions and *iriai* forest commons are of comparable vintage. Compound stress is not a thought experiment for these institutions, it is their history. *(continues in the scoring document)*

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 1.0.

**Islamic Finance / Profit-Sharing Banking: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/if.md#c3-3)

Set against it: in a dual system the Islamic segment funds and hedges alongside the conventional one; the IFSB's 2025 report names heavy commodity-*murabaha* reliance, persistent structural liquidity surpluses, and sectoral concentration as live vulnerabilities; and asset-backing concentrates exposure in property and commodities, which co-move. H.7's 0.5 band — "some resilience from distributed structure, but interconnection still transmits shocks" — applies. Not flagged.

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

Argentina again supplies the clearest counter-evidence: under the compound stress of mass unemployment, a banking-system freeze, and a currency crisis simultaneously, the barter networks' internal controls were overwhelmed.

Degradation stayed well inside the threshold's 20%. The IMF describes ample buffers and strong banks, with non-performing loans at 1.1% (IMF, 2026). *(continues in the scoring document)*

1. *Maintain core functions across ≥3 of 4 compound scenarios with degradation <20%*: not shown. not shown: on the criterion's own poverty measure the configuration's two recent compound shocks did not degrade the function -- the United States' headcount at the Societal Poverty Line was 19.7% in 2008, 19.0% in 2009 and 19.2% in 2010, and 19.8% in 2019, 18.2% in 2020 and 16.7% in 2021 -- but the four scenarios (a 30% fall in GDP among them) lie beyond the record, and housing, services and democratic function under them are not estimated

Read in the adjudication of the outside review (Session 83).

**Universal Basic Services: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/ubs.md#c3-3)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

A real, if incidental, resilience property exists nonetheless: because housing, healthcare, food (for the covered subset), transport, and information are provided directly by the state rather than purchased entirely from private markets funded by wage income, a private-market financial shock or supply disruption does not automatically cut off access to these specific goods the way it would for someone relying entirely on wages to buy market equivalents — the same reasoning already credited to Georgism and Mutual Credit/LETS on this exact criterion. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**MMT + Job Guarantee: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/mmt.md#c3-3)

**Stakeholder Capitalism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/sc.md#c3-3)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> The system has absorbed large shocks without collapse. Growth stayed above 9% through the 2008–09 global crisis, recovered to 8.4% in 2021, and fell to 3.0% in 2022 under zero-COVID lockdowns (World Bank). State-owned banks and capital controls give it tools to roll over and absorb losses that market systems lack, and Stanford SCCEI's analysis argues that the property slump can curb growth for years even without a banking crisis. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**State Capitalism / Qatar (Gulf Rentier-Distributive Statism): 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/qa.md#c3-3)

> Qatar has absorbed three compound shocks in under a decade: *(continues in the scoring document)*

**Libertarian Minarchism: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/lm.md#c3-3)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> **STRUCTURAL FAILURE.** No institutional capacity to respond to compound crises. Every shock would trigger cascading failures with no circuit breakers. Complete vulnerability to systemic risks.

**Doughnut Economics: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/de.md#c3-3)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> The "Get Savvy with Systems" chapter and the City Portrait's four-lens structure (local-social, local-ecological, global-social, global-ecological) explicitly train a jurisdiction to see interconnections and compound risk across social and ecological domains simultaneously — a genuine systems-thinking capacity, evidenced directly by academic case-study work (a 2025 study applying the four-lens framework in Glasgow found it useful for "identify[ing] possible co-benefits and/or unintended consequences of city policies" across a whole system rather than one domain at a time). This is a real, if soft, resilience-relevant capacity — seeing compound risk is a precondition for managing it — but it is a diagnostic lens, not a designed response architecture with redundancy, distributed production, or automatic multi-domain correction of the kind found in the corpus's strongest scorers here (Integral, CCO-PTF-CIP-SZH, Participatory Economics, Degrowth). *(continues in the scoring document)*

**Centrally Planned Socialism: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/cps.md#c3-3)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> **STRUCTURAL FAILURE.** The system ultimately collapsed under compound stresses (technological stagnation, resource depletion, legitimacy crisis, arms race). Rigidity prevented adaptation to multiple simultaneous challenges.

### C3.4 Epistemic Adaptability

Pass Threshold (v2.0): ≥30% parameter adjustability range, policy updates within 6 months of evidence, changes open to challenge and reversal by those affected, zero collapses during parameter adjustments. Revision class M (bar, measure or clause restated).

v2.0: 5 at 1.0, 16 at 0.5, 2 at 0.0. v1: 17 at 1.0, 4 at 0.5, 2 at 0.0.

**CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/cco.md#c3-4)

1. *≥30% parameter adjustability range*: cleared. cleared: the design's documents give adjustable ranges well beyond 30% for several parameters without redesign: the inflation paper's sensitivity analysis cuts the maximum octaves from 8 to 4 (50%) and runs participation from 5% to 35%, keeping inflation below 1.8% and 89% of the welfare gains; the integrated framework sets the conversion multiplier at 1-9x against tested alternatives of 1-5x and 1-12x and a regional adjustment factor of 0.8-1.2; and the scorer's adjustment run on the v5.6 engine moves the Basic Unit by 30% either way (0.7 to 1.3) from year 10
2. *policy updates within 6 months of evidence*: cleared. the design's crisis protocol schedules parameter recalibration within one to six months, and monthly cost-of-living adjustments in an inflation surge
3. *changes open to challenge and reversal by those affected*: cleared. cleared: under the Citizens Internet Portal any citizen can submit a proposal (a signature threshold, a 30-90 day comment period, amendment), and a binding change passes by a 60-67% supermajority with geographic distribution, so those affected can challenge a parameter change and have it reversed by a process that does not run through the officials who made it; referenda move from advisory to binding in the second phase (years 4-7); the PTH charter template adds notice, hearing and appeal rights on every enforcement action
4. *zero collapses during parameter adjustments*: cleared. on the owner's reading (decision 79.3), a collapse is a breakdown that an adjustment causes (runaway prices, a run that fails), not the direct effect of a smaller allowance on the share below the cost of living; on the scorer's adjustment run (the Basic Unit multiplied by 0.7, 0.8, 1.2 or 1.3 from year 10, 500 seeds, Reference) no step causes such a breakdown at either backing: the 1.2 and 1.3 steps add at most 6.8 points to a year's price rise, and the 0.7 and 0.8 steps raise the share below the cost of living (the test fixed before the run counted 339 and 43 of 500 seeds in the release row, largest excess 16.6 points) as a smaller allowance does directly

Read in group 2.5 (Session 57), then raised by the owner's decision 79.3 (Session 79), then clauses 1 and 3 re-read on cited sources (decision 84.3, Session 84).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5. Group 2.5's reading (reading 3.11): on the test fixed before the run, a 20-30% cut in the allowance raises the share below the cost of living by 10 points or more in 339 and 43 of 500 seeds

**Nordic Social Democracy: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/nsd.md#c3-4)

1. *≥30% parameter adjustability range*: cleared. willing to adjust parameters based on research and outcomes
2. *policy updates within 6 months of evidence*: cleared. frequent policy experimentation and evaluation
3. *changes open to challenge and reversal by those affected*: cleared. parameter changes are enacted by elected parliaments (the national turnouts of part (a), NSD C2.4)
4. *zero collapses during parameter adjustments*: short. the financial liberalisation of the 1980s led to systemic banking crises in Finland, Norway and Sweden, among the deepest in advanced economies since the Second World War, with Finland's output falling nearly 15%; Denmark, whose supervision was tightened, avoided a systemic crisis

Read in part (b1) (Session 38).

**Market Socialism: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/ms.md#c3-4)

1. *≥30% parameter adjustability range*: cleared. the pay-ratio cap, a core distributive parameter, moved from 1:3 at founding to 1:4.5 in the 1970s and 1:6 in 1988, a 100% range
2. *policy updates within 6 months of evidence*: cleared. democratic governance enables rapid adaptation to evidence; consistent with the record: in 2009 Eroski's worker-members voted to freeze pay and work 10% more, and a cooperative's assembly agreed rotating leave at 80% of pay, though no source dates a decision against the evidence that called for it
3. *changes open to challenge and reversal by those affected*: cleared. the pay-ratio changes were decided at the cooperatives' General Assemblies, the members themselves (v2.0's wording; ledger I9)
4. *zero collapses during parameter adjustments*: cleared. stated search: no collapse caused by a parameter adjustment located; Fagor Electrodomesticos, the founding cooperative, failed in 2013 after heavy losses in the euro-area and Spanish real-estate crises, carrying debt from its 2005 Brandt acquisition, causes that clear it of an adjustment

Read in part (b1) (Session 38), then adjudicated after the outside review (Session 83).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5. The outside reader's reading: no source dates a policy update within six months of the evidence

**Participatory Economics: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/pe.md#c3-4)

1. *≥30% parameter adjustability range*: cleared. councils adjust proposals based on feedback, every planning cycle
2. *policy updates within 6 months of evidence*: not shown. not shown: 'democratic structure enables rapid evidence-based changes' dates no update against evidence; the planning cycle is annual (the outside reader reads the clause short)
3. *changes open to challenge and reversal by those affected*: cleared. cleared on v2.0's wording: self-managed councils of those affected decide and can reverse
4. *zero collapses during parameter adjustments*: not shown. not shown: no implementation record, and no modelling of adjustments in operation (part (b1), reading 3.1); decision 79.4's rule needs clauses 1 to 3 cleared

Read in part (b1) (Session 38), then raised by the owner's decision 79.4 (Session 79), then lowered to the adjudication's reading by the owner's decision 84.1 (ledger D66, Session 84).

**Integral: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/int.md#c3-4)

1. *≥30% parameter adjustability range*: cleared. cleared: ITC's weighting bands, scarcity coefficients and access-valuation parameters are recalibrated within democratically approved bounds; the white paper's example raises an ecological weight to 1.7-1.8 in drought, a move of 70-80% (reading 3.4.1)
2. *policy updates within 6 months of evidence*: cleared. cleared, as designed: ITC parameters are recalibrated continuously, and CDS Module 10 opens a review when FRS signals or new evidence show outcomes diverging, its example acting on detection six months after a decision (reading 3.4.2)
3. *changes open to challenge and reversal by those affected*: cleared. cleared, as designed: Module 1 takes proposals and objections from participants, decisions are reached by participants' weighted consensus with blocking objections detected, and Module 10 can amend, revoke or reopen a decision: there are no officials on whom the route depends (reading 3.4.3)
4. *zero collapses during parameter adjustments*: cleared. on the rule of decision 79.4: clauses 1 to 3 are cleared (the parameters adjust over a range of 30% or more, updates follow evidence within six months, and those affected can challenge and reverse them), and no implementation record or model located shows a collapse caused by a parameter adjustment, so clause 4 is cleared, as Georgism's clause 4 is read ('no collapse caused by an adjustment located'); the design bounds recalibration within democratically approved ranges and detects drift early (CDS Module 10), and has no implementation record or model of its adjustments

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 71, then raised by the owner's decision 79.4 (Session 79).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5. The blind re-read's reading (Session 71, reading 3.4.4): no implementation record of a parameter adjustment and no modelling of the design's own adjustments in operation, so clause 4 is not shown

**Sovereign Wealth Fund Statism: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/swf.md#c3-4)

1. *≥30% parameter adjustability range*: cleared. the strategic equity share moved from 40% (1998) to 60% (decided June 2007) to 70% (2017): a core parameter moved by half its value; the expected return in the fiscal rule from 4% to 3% (2017)
2. *policy updates within 6 months of evidence*: cleared. the 2017 decisions came on 16 February, eleven weeks after Norges Bank's recommendation of 1 December 2016
3. *changes open to challenge and reversal by those affected*: cleared. cleared on v2.0's wording (contestable through a process that does not depend on the officials who made the change; ledger I9): Alaska's dividend veto of 2016 was challenged in the state Supreme Court (Wielechowski v. State, 2017), and Alaskans may legislate by initiative
4. *zero collapses during parameter adjustments*: cleared. the new equity share held through 2008-2009 without collapse

Read in part (a) (Session 37), then adjudicated after the outside review (Session 83).

**Ostrom-Style Commons Governance: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/os.md#c3-4)

1. *≥30% parameter adjustability range*: cleared. principle 3 is a procedure for changing the rules
2. *policy updates within 6 months of evidence*: cleared. in-period adjustment faster than six months (the acequia scarcity rule)
3. *changes open to challenge and reversal by those affected*: cleared. rule changes governed by those affected
4. *zero collapses during parameter adjustments*: cleared. on the rule of decision 79.4: clauses 1 to 3 are cleared (the parameters adjust over a range of 30% or more, updates follow evidence within six months, and those affected can challenge and reverse them), and no implementation record or model located shows a collapse caused by a parameter adjustment, so clause 4 is cleared, as Georgism's clause 4 is read ('no collapse caused by an adjustment located'); the outright failures and fragile institutions the founding literature records are not attributed to rule changes, and principle 3 is the procedure by which those affected change the rules

Read in part (a) (Session 37), then raised by the owner's decision 79.4 (Session 79).

**Universal Basic Income: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/ubi.md#c3-4)

1. *≥30% parameter adjustability range*: cleared. simple parameter adjustment (benefit level, taxation rates)
2. *policy updates within 6 months of evidence*: short. the longest-running precedent, Alaska's dividend, is set once a year; since the 2017 Supreme Court ruling it competes for annual funding like other programmes; the 2018 percent-of-market-value law came in the third budget year of the deficit that prompted the 2016 veto: annual cycles fall short

**Status Quo Market Capitalism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/sq.md#c3-4)

2. *policy updates within 6 months of evidence*: short. short: the 2008 stimulus came within two months, but zero-COVID was held for about a year after the evidence on Omicron and vaccines and the one-child rule for decades after fertility fell below replacement (reading 3.4.2)

**Centrally Planned Socialism: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/cps.md#c3-4)

1. *≥30% parameter adjustability range*: short. short: state retail prices of staple foods were held from 1962 to 1991 against mounting shortage; each substantive change to allocation rules came as a reorganisation (the regional councils of 1957, undone in 1965; the 1965 reform, wound back; the Law on State Enterprise, 1987), so the range adjustable without redesign was narrow (reading 3.4.1)
2. *policy updates within 6 months of evidence*: short. short: Lysenko's doctrine governed Soviet biology from 1948 to 1964 against the evidence of genetics, and price reform waited decades on the evidence of shortage (reading 3.4.2)
3. *changes open to challenge and reversal by those affected*: short. short: no route to reversal ran outside the Party: no independent courts, no free elections; challenge took the form of strikes met with force (Novocherkassk, 1962; the Baltic coast, 1970) (reading 3.4.3)
4. *zero collapses during parameter adjustments*: short. short: Poland's food price rises of 1970, 1976 and 1980 each brought strikes, the first two were reversed and the third produced Solidarity; the Soviet reforms of 1987-88 preceded the collapse of 1989-91 (reading 3.4.4)

Why 0.0 stands: protocol 2.1, third condition, with D29(a): performance far below the threshold on every clause, documented in several independent implementations (the Soviet Union, Poland), with no credible pathway under the system's own logic -- its adjustments of core parameters ended in unrest or collapse (reading 3.4.5)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 71.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5.

### C3.5 Failure-Mode Transparency

Pass Threshold (v2.0): Failure detection within 1 week, diagnosis success ≥80%, correction success ≥70%, externalization <10% of total costs. Revision class I (indicator named for an unchanged clause and bar).

v2.0: 0 at 1.0, 18 at 0.5, 5 at 0.0. v1: 3 at 1.0, 13 at 0.5, 7 at 0.0.

**CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/cco.md#c3-5)

1. *Failure detection within 1 week*: cleared. Failures legible and diagnosable
2. *diagnosis success ≥80%*: cleared. Failures legible and diagnosable
3. *correction success ≥70%*: not shown. the design specifies monitoring institutions (real-time anomaly detection, AI risk models whose lowest alert level triggers enhanced monitoring with weekly human review, democratic review of major security changes, and a CIP dashboard) but states no share of identified failures successfully corrected; the nearest figure, the CIP paper's conviction probability of 0.62 for detected corruption, is an illustrative input to an expected-cost calculation for one class of failure, and below the bar in any case; the published model represents no detection, diagnosis or correction of failures (reading 3.2)
4. *externalization <10% of total costs*: not shown. no share of total costs externalised is stated or estimated; the rationale's grounds (flows visible on a ledger, member monitoring, a dashboard) concern the visibility of the system's own operations, not costs borne outside it; no design source located prices an environmental cost (none names a carbon tax), and the dashboard lists carbon emissions, energy use and waste reduction as metrics to display, not costs internalised (reading 3.2)

Read in part (b6) (Session 43).

**Nordic Social Democracy: 0.5** (v1 0.0, up). [Evidence](evidence/nsd.md#c3-5)

1. *Failure detection within 1 week*: not shown. the scoring document names no dated record of the entry's most consequential failure, which the measure requires
2. *diagnosis success ≥80%*: not shown. aspirational: no diagnostic record or independent evaluation reporting the share of failures whose cause is established is cited
3. *correction success ≥70%*: not shown. Sweden's Riksrevisionen reported in 2020 that agencies had acted on nearly 85% of its annual-audit recommendations within a year; no rate was located for Denmark, Finland, Iceland or Norway
4. *externalization <10% of total costs*: cleared. implicit fossil fuel subsidies were 0.05% (Finland) to 0.58% (Denmark) of GDP in 2022 in each of the five countries, under 10%

3. *correction success ≥70%*: not shown. recommendations go to democratic deliberation in CDS, and FRS-6 keeps time series to evaluate whether past decisions achieved their stated outcomes; no share of identified failures successfully corrected is stated or modelled, and the review sets democratic deliberation and adaptive implementation beside the bar, not a level (reading 3.2)

**State Capitalism / China (Party-State-Directed Market Economy): 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/cn.md#c3-5)

1. *Failure detection within 1 week*: short. on the dated records the scoring document names, the Henan floods (a State Council investigation found reports of up to 139 dead or missing deliberately withheld) and the epidemic's onset (a doctor's warning met by a police reprimand), identification and correction took months, not a week
2. *diagnosis success ≥80%*: not shown. aspirational: no diagnostic record or independent evaluation reporting the share of failures whose cause is established is cited
3. *correction success ≥70%*: not shown. the National Audit Office reports 92% to 98% of the issues requiring immediate action rectified within the year (2023 to 2025); of all 4,238 issues in the 2024 audit, about 51% were resolved by the December report, the rest on roadmaps; no rate on GAO's basis, recommendations implemented within a stated period, is published
4. *externalization <10% of total costs*: short. implicit fossil fuel subsidies were 10.95% of GDP in 2022, over 10%

Why 0.0 stands: the configuration treats public disclosure of its signature failures as illegitimate (the epidemic warning punished as false information, a censorship directive during the floods, the labour-protest monitor dissolved), and on every clause the record shows it falls short

Read in group 2.4 (Session 56).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5. The published 0.0 and its flag hold; v2.0's clause 4 figure confirms the externalization the rationale names

**State Capitalism / Qatar (Gulf Rentier-Distributive Statism): 0.5** (v1 0.0, up). [Evidence](evidence/qa.md#c3-5)

1. *Failure detection within 1 week*: short. the ILO's report on 2020 found no categorical count of fatal occupational injuries possible, and the authorities do not classify unexplained or heat-related deaths of workers as work-related: failures are left unidentified, not identified within a week
2. *diagnosis success ≥80%*: not shown. aspirational: no diagnostic record or independent evaluation reporting the share of failures whose cause is established is cited
3. *correction success ≥70%*: not shown. no recommendation-implementation rate of the State Audit Bureau was located (its site did not answer this container)
4. *externalization <10% of total costs*: cleared. implicit fossil fuel subsidies were 7.31% of GDP in 2022, under 10% (explicit subsidies, undercharging for supply costs, are a different quantity)

Read in group 2.4 (Session 56).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.0. Published 0.0 on a continuous estimate of 0.2 to 0.35; with clause 4 cleared on the measure v2.0 names, 2.1's 0.0 conditions do not hold for the criterion as a whole (decision 53.2), and the unit rises to 0.5 (reading 3.6)

**Libertarian Minarchism: 0.5** (v1 0.0, up). [Evidence](evidence/lm.md#c3-5)

1. *Failure detection within 1 week*: not shown. losses and bankruptcy make a firm's failure visible to its owners and creditors; no monitoring rule stating how soon a failure is identified is specified or cited
2. *diagnosis success ≥80%*: not shown. aspirational: no diagnostic record or independent evaluation reporting the share of failures whose cause is established is cited
3. *correction success ≥70%*: not shown. no audit institution or correction record for systemic failures is specified; no correction record, and no share of identified failures corrected, is stated or cited
4. *externalization <10% of total costs*: not shown. market externalities are left to claims by identifiable victims; no externalized cost is projected as a share of total costs

Read in group 2.4 (Session 56).

**Doughnut Economics: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/de.md#c3-5)

1. *Failure detection within 1 week*: not shown. City Portraits surface shortfalls and overshoots publicly, but on a reporting cycle, not within a week of a failure; no dated record is named
2. *diagnosis success ≥80%*: not shown. aspirational: no diagnostic record or independent evaluation reporting the share of failures whose cause is established is cited
3. *correction success ≥70%*: not shown. correction is left to each jurisdiction's political process; an assessment found Amsterdam's implementation 'incipient and limited'; no share corrected is stated
4. *externalization <10% of total costs*: not shown. the framework reports ecological overshoot but prices none of it; no externalized cost is projected as a share of total costs

Read in group 2.4 (Session 56).

**Centrally Planned Socialism: 0.0** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/cps.md#c3-5)

1. *Failure detection within 1 week*: short. short: shortages and queues were visible to all, but failure information was suppressed: the Kyshtym accident of 1957 was acknowledged only in 1989, the famine of 1932-33 was officially denied, and Chernobyl's release was announced only after radiation was detected in Sweden (28 April 1986)
2. *diagnosis success ≥80%*: not shown. not shown: no diagnostic record or independent evaluation reporting the share of failures whose cause was established
3. *correction success ≥70%*: short. correction came through eventual collapse and reform, in every Soviet-type economy: a failure replicated across independent implementations (D29(a))
4. *externalization <10% of total costs*: not shown. not estimated: no externalised cost is projected as a share of total costs (the outside reader, closed-book, puts unpriced pollution and energy far above 10% of GDP)

Why 0.0 stands: the system actively suppressed failure information (protocol 2.1; the 0.0 band: active suppression of failure information), the ground on which China's C3.5 is 0.0

Read in part (a) (Session 37), then adjudicated after the outside review (Session 83).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5. The published reading: failures such as shortages were visible, and correction came through reform

**Market Socialism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/ms.md#c4-1)

1. *Debt-to-GDP <80%*: not shown. not shown: the design's sources (worker cooperatives in competitive markets, Mondragon and the cooperative movements) state no public-finance rule or projected debt path (reading 4.1.1)
2. *positive wealth transfer to next generation*: cleared. cleared, by rule: the cooperatives retain their net surplus as capital -- at least 20% to an indivisible collective reserve and the rest, after the social fund, to members' capital accounts that stay invested until the member leaves (Mondragon), at least 30% to an indivisible legal reserve (Italian Civil Code, art. 2545-quater) -- so the productive sector's net saving is positive whenever it earns a surplus (reading 4.1.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 72.

**Participatory Economics: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/pe.md#c4-1)

1. *Debt-to-GDP <80%*: not shown. not shown: there is no state borrowing in the market sense, but neighbourhood consumption councils authorise households' borrowing and saving, and consumers, councils and federations carry the year's differences as debt or savings; no rule bounds the federations' debts and no path is projected (reading 4.1.1)
2. *positive wealth transfer to next generation*: not shown. not shown: each year's plan runs inside an investment plan that sets the capacity added to each industry, but the design states no net accumulation rule or projection (reading 4.1.2)

Read in part (b6) (Session 43) and re-read blind in Session 72.

**Integral: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/int.md#c4-1)

1. *Debt-to-GDP <80%*: cleared. cleared, by construction: the design has no instrument of borrowing -- time credits are non-transferable, decay over time and are extinguished on use, so nothing can be lent or owed -- and no public debt arises within it (reading 4.1.1)
2. *positive wealth transfer to next generation*: short. short, by the design's logic: success is measured 'rather than growth or capital accumulation', and 'prosperity is defined by sufficiency and reliability, not surplus accumulation'; the design sustains its material cycles but states no net accumulation (reading 4.1.2)

Read in part (b6) (Session 43) and re-read blind in Session 72.

**Sovereign Wealth Fund Statism: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/swf.md#c4-1)

1. *Debt-to-GDP <80%*: out of reach. out of reach: the mechanism governs no public borrowing, so the debt ratio is the host economy's (reading 4.1.1); the withdrawal rule governs what the fund pays into the budget, not the rest of the government's borrowing (Norway's gross debt, 54.0% of GDP in 2025, is the host's)
2. *positive wealth transfer to next generation*: short. short: the mechanism converts a non-recurring revenue into a held fund, a positive transfer while withdrawals stay within the return, as under Norway's rule; Timor-Leste withdrew above its Estimated Sustainable Income in 2008-12 and every year from 2014, and the IMF (2017) projects the fund's depletion, so the transfer rests on the rule's observance, not on the mechanism (reading 4.1.2)

Read in part (b6) (Session 43) and re-read blind in Session 72.

**Ostrom-Style Commons Governance: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/os.md#c4-1)

1. *Debt-to-GDP <80%*: out of reach. out of reach: the mechanism governs no public borrowing, so the debt ratio is the host economy's (reading 4.1.1)
2. *positive wealth transfer to next generation*: out of reach. out of reach: sustained yield preserves a local resource stock, which is the ecological transfer (C4.2's), not national saving (readings 4.1.2, 4.1.4)

Read in part (b6) (Session 43) and re-read blind in Session 72.

**Universal Basic Income: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/ubi.md#c4-1)

1. *Debt-to-GDP <80%*: out of reach. out of reach: the mechanism governs no public borrowing, so the debt ratio is the host economy's (reading 4.1.1); the funding (income taxation in the leading design, other variants not fixed by the mechanism) is a choice the transfer does not make
2. *positive wealth transfer to next generation*: out of reach. out of reach: an income transfer, which governs neither saving nor investment (reading 4.1.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 72.

**Degrowth Economics: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/dg.md#c4-1)

1. *Debt-to-GDP <80%*: not shown. not shown: the degrowth literature argues against growth that is driven by debt and for audits of illegitimate debt, but states no projected path of the public debt ratio (reading 4.1.1)
2. *positive wealth transfer to next generation*: short. short, by the design's logic: the transition its literature describes ends in a steady state, constant stocks of people and artifacts maintained by a low throughput (Daly, 1991; Kerschner, 2010), in which net accumulation is zero rather than positive; preservation of the natural stock is C4.2's (readings 4.1.2, 4.1.4)

Read in part (b6) (Session 43) and re-read blind in Session 72.

**Georgism / Land Value Tax (LVT + Citizen's Dividend): 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/geo.md#c4-1)

1. *Debt-to-GDP <80%*: out of reach. out of reach: the mechanism governs no public borrowing, so the debt ratio is the host economy's (reading 4.1.1); the dividend is paid only from revenue left after public goods, which bounds the dividend, not the debt
2. *positive wealth transfer to next generation*: out of reach. out of reach: the tax captures land rent and the dividend distributes it rather than investing it (the scoring document's own contrast with a held corpus); national saving is the host's (reading 4.1.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 72.

**Islamic Finance / Profit-Sharing Banking: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/if.md#c4-1)

1. *Debt-to-GDP <80%*: out of reach. out of reach: the mechanism governs no public borrowing, so the debt ratio is the host economy's (reading 4.1.1); sovereign sukuk are market practice outside the profit-sharing contract set the scoring document scores
2. *positive wealth transfer to next generation*: out of reach. out of reach: profit-sharing accounts are an accumulating household corpus, a channel for saving, not a rule that raises net national saving (reading 4.1.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 72.

**Mutual Credit / LETS: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/mc.md#c4-1)

1. *Debt-to-GDP <80%*: out of reach. out of reach: the mechanism governs no public borrowing, so the debt ratio is the host economy's (reading 4.1.1)
2. *positive wealth transfer to next generation*: out of reach. out of reach: balances sum to exactly zero by construction, so the mechanism creates no net financial saving and does not govern real saving (reading 4.1.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 72.

**State Capitalism / Singapore (GLC Developmental Capitalism): 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/sg.md#c4-1)

1. *Debt-to-GDP <80%*: short. short: general government gross debt is 165.7% of GDP in 2025 and between 105.9% and 170.4% in every year from 2016; the securities are issued to develop the debt market and their proceeds invested, and the government's assets exceed its debt, but v2.0's measure is gross debt and governs (reading 4.1.1)
2. *positive wealth transfer to next generation*: cleared. cleared: net national saving is positive in every year of 2012 to 2021, 25.7% to 34.9% of GNI (reading 4.1.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 72.

**Status Quo Market Capitalism: 0.5** (v1 0.0, up). [Evidence](evidence/sq.md#c4-1)

1. *Debt-to-GDP <80%*: short. far short: general government gross debt is 124.4% of GDP in 2025, between 106.5% and 132.6% in every year from 2016, and 145.0% on the WEO's projection to 2031; no rule bounds it (reading 4.1.1)
2. *positive wealth transfer to next generation*: cleared. cleared, narrowly: net national saving is positive in every year of 2012 to 2021 (1.4% to 3.8% of GNI) and, on BEA's net saving, of 2016 to 2025 (lowest 0.9%) (reading 4.1.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 72.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.0. 0.5: debt is far above the bar; the positive transfer is small but positive in every year of the window

**Universal Basic Services: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/ubs.md#c4-1)

1. *Debt-to-GDP <80%*: out of reach. out of reach: the mechanism governs no public borrowing, so the debt ratio is the host economy's (reading 4.1.1); the services are costed at about 2.3% of GDP and funded from taxation, so they add no debt by design
2. *positive wealth transfer to next generation*: out of reach. out of reach: service provision governs neither national saving nor investment beyond its own stock (reading 4.1.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 72.

**MMT + Job Guarantee: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/mmt.md#c4-1)

1. *Debt-to-GDP <80%*: short. short: functional finance sets the deficit by employment and inflation, not by a debt ratio, and the framework's own account holds the debt's burden on the next generation to be a myth (Kelton, 2020, the third of six); the job guarantee is financed by the currency issuer's spending, so no rule bounds the ratio (reading 4.1.1)
2. *positive wealth transfer to next generation*: not shown. not shown: the sectoral balances let public deficits meet private saving desires; no projection of net national saving is stated (reading 4.1.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 72.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.0. 0.5: clause 1 rejected by the framework's logic, clause 2 not addressed; 4.1.3 asks both for 0.0

**Fully Automated Luxury Communism: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/falc.md#c4-1)

1. *Debt-to-GDP <80%*: not shown. not shown: the design's sources call for public investment in energy and automation and an end to austerity, but state no projected path of the public debt ratio (reading 4.1.1)
2. *positive wealth transfer to next generation*: not shown. not shown: the investment programme is stated, net national saving is not projected (reading 4.1.2)

Read in part (b6) (Session 43) and re-read blind in Session 72.

**Stakeholder Capitalism: 0.5** (v1 0.0, up). [Evidence](evidence/sc.md#c4-1)

1. *Debt-to-GDP <80%*: short. far short: the entry keeps the host market economy's public finances unmodified and adds voluntary stakeholder governance and ESG metrics: in the economy it reforms gross debt is 124.4% of GDP in 2025, 145.0% on the WEO's projection to 2031 (reading 4.1.1)
2. *positive wealth transfer to next generation*: cleared. cleared, narrowly, on the host's figures: net saving is positive in every year of 2016 to 2025 (lowest 0.9% of GNI, BEA); the reform adds no saving rule (reading 4.1.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 72.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.0. 0.5: the host's readings, which the reform does not change

**State Capitalism / China (Party-State-Directed Market Economy): 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/cn.md#c4-1)

1. *Debt-to-GDP <80%*: short. short: general government gross debt is 98.6% of GDP in 2025, above 80% since 2023, and 122.1% on the WEO's projection to 2031 (reading 4.1.1)
2. *positive wealth transfer to next generation*: cleared. cleared: net national saving is positive in every year of 2012 to 2021, 17.1% to 26.7% of GNI (reading 4.1.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 72.

**State Capitalism / Qatar (Gulf Rentier-Distributive Statism): 1.0** (v1 0.5, up). [Evidence](evidence/qa.md#c4-1)

2. *positive wealth transfer to next generation*: cleared. cleared: net national saving is positive in every year of 2012 to 2021, 24.5% to 47.9% of GNI, and stays positive net of gas and oil depletion (adjusted net savings 17.9% to 33.5%), so the check finds no sign disagreement (reading 4.1.2)

**Libertarian Minarchism: 0.5** (v1 0.0, up). [Evidence](evidence/lm.md#c4-1)

1. *Debt-to-GDP <80%*: cleared. cleared, by stated rule: 'Government should not incur debt, which burdens future generations without their consent', with a balanced-budget amendment balanced by cutting expenditure, in a state confined to protecting rights (reading 4.1.1)

1. *Debt-to-GDP <80%*: not shown. not shown: the design is agnostic about growth and proposes redesigning money and finance, but states no projected path of the public debt ratio (reading 4.1.1)
2. *positive wealth transfer to next generation*: not shown. not shown: regenerative design concerns the natural stock, which is C4.2's; no net national saving is projected (readings 4.1.2, 4.1.4)

Read in part (b6) (Session 43) and re-read blind in Session 72.

**Centrally Planned Socialism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/cps.md#c4-1)

1. *Debt-to-GDP <80%*: short. short, on the path: the rouble state debt the USSR Ministry of Finance reported was to reach 470 billion roubles by the end of 1990, 74% of 1989's net material product and about half of the 930 billion roubles of GDP Ofer gives for 1989, so the level was below the bar; but the budget deficit, 2.3% of GDP in 1981-85, was 13.3% in the original plan for 1989 and 10.1% in the revised one, and a debt growing by a tenth of GDP a year is not kept below the bar by any rule of the configuration (reading 4.1.1)
2. *positive wealth transfer to next generation*: cleared. cleared: investment rose from 22% of GNP in 1960 to 33% in 1987 and the capital stock grew faster than output under extensive growth, so net accumulation was positive throughout; Ofer counts the backlog of infrastructure, technology and services and the obsolete equipment never retired as an internal debt in real terms, which the measure's depreciation does not see (reading 4.1.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 72.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 1.0. 0.5: positive net accumulation, a debt below the bar in level but on a path through it

### C4.2 Ecological Compliance

Pass Threshold (v2.0): Consumption-based carbon emissions falling ≥3.8% a year, biodiversity neutral or positive, resource extraction ≤ regeneration, no more than two of the four nationally downscaled planetary boundaries transgressed. Revision class M (bar, measure or clause restated).

v2.0: 0 at 1.0, 17 at 0.5, 6 at 0.0. v1: 7 at 1.0, 8 at 0.5, 8 at 0.0.

**CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/cco.md#c4-2)

1. *Consumption-based carbon emissions falling ≥3.8% a year*: not shown. the audited phrase, a 35-45% reduction trajectory with a carbon tax, states a size without a horizon and so no rate (reading 3.2); the design's model reports total emissions 45% below an unstated baseline trajectory over 20 years, of which consumption-driven emissions fall 24%, which fixes no absolute rate without the baseline (against a flat one it is about 2.9% a year), and the roadmap's carbon targets (15-20% in year 1 to 70-80% in year 7) are targets in a table that does not say whose emissions it covers; the published model represents no emissions (reading 3.4)
2. *biodiversity neutral or positive*: not shown. biodiversity appears in the design's sources as a metric to monitor (environmental health: emissions, biodiversity, regeneration) and, in a glossary example, as one of the stewardship results a Public Trust Foundation may reward in Acre Equity; no projection of pressure on species or habitat under the design is stated, modelled or located (reading 3.3)
3. *resource extraction ≤ regeneration*: not shown. the roadmap's resource table projects cuts of 60-90% in the paper, energy and water use it lists without saying whose use they are, and no comparison of resource use with regeneration or biocapacity is stated; the published model represents no resource flows, as part (b6) found for C4.1's regeneration clause
4. *no more than two of the four nationally downscaled planetary boundaries transgressed*: not shown. no planetary boundary is named in the design's sources, which list regeneration among the environmental metrics to monitor; no boundary is assessed for the design

Read in part (b7) (Session 47).

**Nordic Social Democracy: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/nsd.md#c4-2)

1. *Consumption-based carbon emissions falling ≥3.8% a year*: short. short: consumption-based CO2 changed by -1.36%, -3.46%, -3.12%, -1.60% a year over 2013 to 2023 (Denmark, Finland, Norway, Sweden), none at the bar's -3.8%; the configuration clears only if each does (reading 4.2.1)
2. *biodiversity neutral or positive*: cleared. cleared: the Red List Index is not below its 2016 level in 2025 in all four (0.97453 to 0.97492; 0.98038 to 0.98100; 0.95113 to 0.95134; 0.98979 to 0.99037) (reading 4.2.2)
3. *resource extraction ≤ regeneration*: short. short: the footprint of consumption is 4.25, 3.68, 3.50, 3.83 times world biocapacity per person in 2015 (reading 4.2.3)
4. *no more than two of the four nationally downscaled planetary boundaries transgressed*: short. short: Denmark 3 of four; Finland 3 of four; Norway 3 of four; Sweden 3 of four transgressed (phosphorus, nitrogen and land-system change above the boundary; blue water within) (reading 4.2.4)

Read in group 2.10 (Session 64) and re-read blind in Session 73.

**Market Socialism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/ms.md#c4-2)

1. *Consumption-based carbon emissions falling ≥3.8% a year*: not shown. not shown: democratic firms that maximise income per worker lack capital's expansionary imperative, and social control of investment could direct it, but no cap or emissions path is stated (reading 4.2.1)
2. *biodiversity neutral or positive*: not shown. not shown: no pressure on species is projected (reading 4.2.2)
3. *resource extraction ≤ regeneration*: not shown. not shown: no footprint is projected (reading 4.2.3)
4. *no more than two of the four nationally downscaled planetary boundaries transgressed*: not shown. not shown: no boundary is projected (reading 4.2.4)

Read in group 2.10 (Session 64) and re-read blind in Session 73.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.0. 0.5: a structural damper on the growth imperative, no limit stated

**Participatory Economics: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/pe.md#c4-2)

1. *Consumption-based carbon emissions falling ≥3.8% a year*: not shown. the model's pollution damage revealing mechanism prices pollution inside annual planning and, under the model's assumptions, reduces it to efficient levels; an efficient level is not a rate of reduction, and no emissions path is stated or cited for the model, which has no implementation, as part (b6) found for C4.1's carbon clause (reading 3.4)
2. *biodiversity neutral or positive*: not shown. the planning councils can constrain production and consumption within ecological limits and price ecological damage; no projection of pressure on species or habitat under the design is stated, modelled or located (reading 3.3)
3. *resource extraction ≤ regeneration*: cleared. sustainable resource use
4. *no more than two of the four nationally downscaled planetary boundaries transgressed*: cleared. within ecological limits

Read in part (b7) (Session 47).

**Integral: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/int.md#c4-2)

1. *Consumption-based carbon emissions falling ≥3.8% a year*: not shown. OAD computes embodied carbon and lifecycle impacts for each design, and CDS's context model carries emissions proxies into deliberation; no emissions path for the economy is stated or modelled, as part (b6) found for C4.1's carbon clause (reading 3.4)
2. *biodiversity neutral or positive*: not shown. the white paper names biodiversity collapse among the crises of market systems and couples production to ecological thresholds, footprints and risk metrics in CDS, OAD and FRS; no projection of pressure on species or habitat under the design is stated, modelled or located (reading 3.3)
3. *resource extraction ≤ regeneration*: not shown. the design states that production stays within planetary boundaries through real-time ecological feedback, with material footprints computed for designs and FRS flagging constraint violations; no footprint of consumption against biocapacity, or other comparison of extraction with regeneration, is estimated, and the system has no implementation
4. *no more than two of the four nationally downscaled planetary boundaries transgressed*: cleared. enforce planetary-boundary compliance

Read in part (b7) (Session 47).

2. *biodiversity neutral or positive*: not shown. biodiversity breakdown is one of the nine boundaries of the framework's ecological ceiling, and its authors' monitoring measures the world's overshoot of it; the ceiling is a goalpost the economy should respect, and no projection of pressure on species or habitat under the design is stated, modelled or located (reading 3.3)
3. *resource extraction ≤ regeneration*: not shown. the national doughnut accounts compare consumption-based footprints with per-person boundaries as a diagnosis; by the scope decision no reduction of extraction to regeneration is delivered by the framework or estimated for its adopters

2. *biodiversity neutral or positive*: short. short: the irrigation plan for the Amu Darya and Syr Darya halved the Aral Sea and ended its fishery, most of its native fish species lost, the defining case of the planned economy's pressure on habitat (reading 4.2.2)
3. *resource extraction ≤ regeneration*: not shown. not shown: the footprint accounts carry the USSR to 1991, but the figure was not cached (reading 4.2.3)
4. *no more than two of the four nationally downscaled planetary boundaries transgressed*: not shown. not shown: the downscaled boundary accounts begin in 1992 (reading 4.2.4)

Why 0.0 stands: the plan's gross-output targets drove extensive growth in throughput with no absolute limit, emissions rising every decade to the collapse and the Aral Sea destroyed by the plan itself; the emission norms of 1980 were overridden by the plan (reading 4.2.5; D29(a), the same pattern across the Eastern Bloc's planned economies)

Read in group 2.10 (Session 64) and re-read blind in Session 73.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5. 0.0: far below on the clauses read, the plan's own logic expanding throughput

### C4.3 Group Equity

Pass Threshold (v2.0): Disparity reduction ≥5 percentage points every 5 years, convergence trajectory toward <20% disparities within 30 years, on each declared axis, non-citizen residents included. Revision class M (bar, measure or clause restated).

v2.0: 0 at 1.0, 19 at 0.5, 4 at 0.0. v1: 7 at 1.0, 13 at 0.5, 3 at 0.0.

**CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/cco.md#c4-3)

1. *Disparity reduction ≥5 percentage points every 5 years*: not shown. the audited phrase, universal provision that addresses disparities without stigma, is the phrase re-read for Universal Basic Income: access to the benefit, not a fall in a gap; targeting disadvantaged communities first, with 150%+ proportional benefits during rollout, is a benefit flow, which v2.0's measurement counts as evidence for the trajectory, not a separate test, and it states no gap or rate; the design's published model represents no groups, and its research hub projects no gap; no projection of the gap between groups' median household incomes on any declared axis is stated, modelled or located, so neither the rate nor the convergence is shown (reading 3.3)
2. *convergence trajectory toward <20% disparities within 30 years*: not shown. the audited phrase, trafficking vulnerability eliminated through unconditional security, is an outcome on another measure, not the path of a household income gap, and aesthetic multipliers that value historically devalued creative work are a mechanism; no projection of the gap between groups' median household incomes on any declared axis is stated, modelled or located, so neither the rate nor the convergence is shown (reading 3.3)

Read in part (b8) (Session 49).

**Nordic Social Democracy: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/nsd.md#c4-3)

1. *Disparity reduction ≥5 percentage points every 5 years*: short. the entry's own text names immigrant and minority communities, so immigrant background is a declared axis, read by country of birth and by citizenship (non-EU against natives or nationals); in each of the four countries a gap above 20% falls by less than 5 points in five years on both measures: {ROWS} (three-year means, 2023-2025 against 2018-2020; the trend is the least-squares fit over the ten editions, per five years) (reading 3.4)
2. *convergence trajectory toward <20% disparities within 30 years*: not shown. the audited phrase, the strongest performance on gender equity globally, claims the gender axis, not the immigrant axis the entry's text declares; on the ten-edition trends the immigrant-axis gaps above 20% reach 20% by {LAST} except Denmark's by citizenship, whose trend rises ({DKTREND} points per five years) although its three-year mean fell; series this noisy do not establish a convergence trajectory (reading 3.4)

Read in part (b8) (Session 49).

2. *convergence trajectory toward <20% disparities within 30 years*: short. no trajectory toward 20%: the Black-white non-Hispanic gap has stayed between 34.8% and 44.7% in all 53 editions since 1972 (42.5% in 1972, 38.0% in 2025), and the Black-Asian gap widened by 7.7 points from 2002 to 2025; at the long-run rate the 20% level is not reached within 30 years on either comparison (reading 3.3)

Why 0.0 stands: the best evidence shows performance far below the threshold with no credible pathway under the system's own logic (protocol 2.1): on the measure v2.0 names the gap between the least and most advantaged groups is above 50% and wider than in 2002, the Black-white gap has not left the band of 35% to 45% in 53 years, and the system's group-directed remedies are being withdrawn (race-conscious admissions in 2023, federal contractors' affirmative action in 2025); the 0.0 band's own example

2. *convergence trajectory toward <20% disparities within 30 years*: not shown. as for clause 1: no projection of the gap's path is stated or located (part (b8), reading 3.3)

**MMT + Job Guarantee: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/mmt.md#c4-3)

1. *Disparity reduction ≥5 percentage points every 5 years*: not shown. the audited phrase, eliminating discrimination in hiring through the government as employer of last resort, is a process; the programme's proponents argue that it would effectively eliminate the racial unemployment gap and set an economy-wide floor on compensation, but a gap in unemployment is not a gap in median household income, and no projection of the gap between groups' median household incomes on any declared axis is stated, modelled or located, so neither the rate nor the convergence is shown (reading 3.3)
2. *convergence trajectory toward <20% disparities within 30 years*: not shown. the rationale's care and community-service jobs address gendered devaluation of labour, a mechanism; no projection of the gap between groups' median household incomes on any declared axis is stated, modelled or located, so neither the rate nor the convergence is shown (reading 3.3)

Read in part (b8) (Session 49).

**Fully Automated Luxury Communism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/falc.md#c4-3)

1. *Disparity reduction ≥5 percentage points every 5 years*: not shown. universal abundance narrows material gaps by its form, but governance and historical inequities are unaddressed by the entry's account; no projection of the gap between groups' median household incomes on any axis, from a model or calibrated components, is stated or located, so neither the rate nor the convergence is shown (part (b8), reading 3.3)
2. *convergence trajectory toward <20% disparities within 30 years*: not shown. as for clause 1: no projection of the gap's path is stated or located (part (b8), reading 3.3)

Read in group 2.8 (Session 61).

**Stakeholder Capitalism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/sc.md#c4-3)

1. *Disparity reduction ≥5 percentage points every 5 years*: not shown. diversity initiatives and hiring targets are group-directed but address representation, not household income gaps, and the host economy's gaps are not credited to the mechanism (protocol 3.2); no projection of the gap between groups' median household incomes on any axis, from a model or calibrated components, is stated or located, so neither the rate nor the convergence is shown (part (b8), reading 3.3)
2. *convergence trajectory toward <20% disparities within 30 years*: not shown. as for clause 1: no projection of the gap's path is stated or located (part (b8), reading 3.3)

Read in group 2.8 (Session 61).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.0. 0.5 holds: a group-directed mechanism of partial reach (reading 3.2); flagged now

**State Capitalism / China (Party-State-Directed Market Economy): 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/cn.md#c4-3)

1. *Disparity reduction ≥5 percentage points every 5 years*: not shown. no national household survey publishes median household income by ethnic group (the National Bureau of Statistics publishes by urban and rural residence and by region), so no gap on the ethnic axis can be measured, as for Centrally Planned Socialism (part (b8), reading 3.5) (reading 3.5)
2. *convergence trajectory toward <20% disparities within 30 years*: not shown. no series exists from which a convergence on the ethnic axis could be projected; top-level gender representation has fallen since 2022 (reading 3.5)

Why 0.0 stands: the system excludes the criterion's concern for the minorities most affected (protocol 2.1): coercive state action targeting them, which the UN human rights office found may constitute crimes against humanity, and an assimilationist ethnic law of 2026, a harm beyond the formal neutrality of the 0.0 band

Read in group 2.8 (Session 61).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5. 0.0 holds

**State Capitalism / Qatar (Gulf Rentier-Distributive Statism): 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/qa.md#c4-3)

1. *Disparity reduction ≥5 percentage points every 5 years*: not shown. the 2012/13 household survey reports average monthly household income of QR 72,700 for Qatari households and QR 24,400 for non-Qatari households, a gap of 66.4% on means, with workers in collective housing outside the survey; one survey gives no rate (reading 3.5)
2. *convergence trajectory toward <20% disparities within 30 years*: not shown. no series is located from which a convergence on the citizenship axis could be projected (reading 3.5)

Why 0.0 stands: the system excludes the criterion's concern (protocol 2.1): rights and rent-funded benefits are allocated by nationality to the most advantaged legal group, the reverse of the flow the criterion asks for, and v2.0's scope counts the non-citizen residents, most of the population, whom the allocation excludes

Read in group 2.8 (Session 61).

**Libertarian Minarchism: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/lm.md#c4-3)

1. *Disparity reduction ≥5 percentage points every 5 years*: not shown. formal equality before the law only: the minimal state's functions exclude any corrective measure, and no mechanism bears on a gap between groups; no projection of the gap between groups' median household incomes on any axis, from a model or calibrated components, is stated or located, so neither the rate nor the convergence is shown (part (b8), reading 3.3)
2. *convergence trajectory toward <20% disparities within 30 years*: not shown. as for clause 1: no projection of the gap's path is stated or located (part (b8), reading 3.3)

Why 0.0 stands: the system excludes the criterion's concern (protocol 2.1): its own logic confines the state to protecting rights and rules out any corrective flow, so historical disparities persist unchallenged, as its rationale says

Read in group 2.8 (Session 61).

**Doughnut Economics: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/de.md#c4-3)

1. *Disparity reduction ≥5 percentage points every 5 years*: not shown. gender equality is one of the social foundation's twelve dimensions and unpaid care work is engaged directly, which bears on the gender axis; no reparative mechanism for racial disparities is located; no projection of the gap between groups' median household incomes on any axis, from a model or calibrated components, is stated or located, so neither the rate nor the convergence is shown (part (b8), reading 3.3)
2. *convergence trajectory toward <20% disparities within 30 years*: not shown. as for clause 1: no projection of the gap's path is stated or located (part (b8), reading 3.3)

Read in group 2.8 (Session 61).

**Centrally Planned Socialism: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/cps.md#c4-3)

1. *Disparity reduction ≥5 percentage points every 5 years*: not shown. the rationale is silent on the rate, as the audit found; the Soviet statistics office did not publish the size distribution of household income from the 1920s until 1989, and then by republic only for 1988 and 1990, two years apart, so no source can show a gap between nationalities' median household incomes falling 5 points in five years; the 1988 distributions show the southern republics' per capita household incomes well below the northern ones' (reading 3.5)
2. *convergence trajectory toward <20% disparities within 30 years*: not shown. the audited phrase, women's high labour force participation, education and leadership roles, is an outcome on another measure, not a trajectory of household income gaps; for the same reason no series by nationality exists from which a convergence could be projected (readings 3.2 and 3.5)

Read in part (b8) (Session 49).

### C4.4 Power Distribution

Pass Threshold (v2.0): Democratic accountability for ≥80% of major decisions, removal/replacement mechanisms functional. Revision class M (bar, measure or clause restated).

v2.0: 3 at 1.0, 15 at 0.5, 5 at 0.0. v1: 6 at 1.0, 7 at 0.5, 10 at 0.0.

**CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/cco.md#c4-4)

1. *Democratic accountability for ≥80% of major decisions*: not shown. the audited phrase, CIP provides direct democratic power, does not claim the defined condition, so the clause is estimated: CIP administers the currency and manages democratic voting for the Public Trust Housing and Public Trust Foundation decisions, and the Public Trust Foundations (grocers, restaurants, utilities and transit accepting basic units) are governed democratically by employees, customers and community representatives; but the design's sources say this network coexists with private markets rather than replacing them and complements rather than displaces private operations, its corporate paper describes a shift from shareholder to stakeholder models without saying who would elect the boards of transformed corporations, and the participation figures it states are for housing (a 50% market share target) and for merchants within designated zones (55% before synergy effects activate), not for employment; no source states or projects that the accountable organizations would employ 80% of workers
2. *removal/replacement mechanisms functional*: cleared. as part (b7) recorded: Public Trust Housing leadership has term limits, mandatory rotation and recall elections, and the merit juries are selected at random and rotated

Read in decision 48.3 (Session 48).

**Nordic Social Democracy: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/nsd.md#c4-4)

1. *Democratic accountability for ≥80% of major decisions*: short. V-Dem's Regimes of the World classes Denmark, Finland, Norway and Sweden as liberal democracies, so their public sectors count; on the most generous count (all of general government, every central-government state-owned enterprise, the self-employed, and every cooperative, mutual, association and foundation) the share of employment in organizations whose decisions answer to those they affect is at most {DK} in Denmark, {FI} in Finland and {SE} in Sweden, and {NO} in Norway before its social economy, which would have to employ {NOGAP} of workers, {RATIO} times the highest share in the EU-28 ({SEMAX}); the rest work in companies whose boards shareholders elect, employees electing a minority (Sweden two or three members, a quarter to a third of the board; Norway and Denmark a third; Finland as agreed, in companies of 150 or more); short of 80% in each, by at least {GAP} points (decision 48.3(d))
2. *removal/replacement mechanisms functional*: cleared. parliamentary removal functions: Sweden's prime minister lost a confidence vote on 21 June 2021, as part (a) found

Read in decision 48.3 (Session 48).

**Market Socialism: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/ms.md#c4-4)

1. *Democratic accountability for ≥80% of major decisions*: cleared. the audited phrase, economic power distributed through democratic ownership, claims the defined condition: the system places enterprises in the ownership of those who work in them and removes the employer-employee hierarchy, and in its principal component each cooperative's worker-members elect the governing council, one member one vote, and can dismiss it in general assembly (all of the system's employment)
2. *removal/replacement mechanisms functional*: cleared. as part (b7) recorded: administrators serve two to five years and the general assembly may dismiss them and elect their replacements in the same session

Read in decision 48.3 (Session 48), then adjudicated after the outside review (Session 83).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5. The outside reader's reading: the design's family includes variants (Lange and Lerner's state firms, Roemer's coupon boards) that place firm control elsewhere (protocol 4.2)

**Participatory Economics: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/pe.md#c4-4)

1. *Democratic accountability for ≥80% of major decisions*: cleared. the audited phrase, comprehensive economic democracy, claims the defined condition: the model has no owners of capital and no managerial hierarchy, places all production in workers' councils that decide by their members' votes and all consumption in consumers' councils, and federations of councils they elect decide wider matters, so the investment, production and employment decisions of every workplace are taken by those who work there or by those they elect (all of the design's employment)
2. *removal/replacement mechanisms functional*: cleared. as part (b7) recorded: workers' and consumers' councils elect recallable and rotated representatives to their federations

Read in decision 48.3 (Session 48).

**Integral: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/int.md#c4-4)

1. *Democratic accountability for ≥80% of major decisions*: cleared. the audited phrase, broad democratic accountability, claims the defined condition: the design delegates no managerial authority, decisions are taken by participants in CDS at every scale with local autonomy preserved, production is coordinated by rotating teams that coordinate rather than command, and ITC's non-accumulability keeps wealth from converting into power, so the investment, production and employment decisions are taken by those they affect (all of the design's employment)
2. *removal/replacement mechanisms functional*: cleared. as part (b7) recorded: decisions taken in CDS can be reaffirmed, amended, revoked or reopened by its review module, and no managerial authority is delegated

Read in decision 48.3 (Session 48).

**Sovereign Wealth Fund Statism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/swf.md#c4-4)

1. *Democratic accountability for ≥80% of major decisions*: out of reach. out of reach: the fund manages a portfolio of minority stakes and votes as a shareholder; it governs no organization's employment decisions beyond its own (reading 4.4.1)
2. *removal/replacement mechanisms functional*: out of reach. out of reach: the mechanism governs no removal of legislators, executives or managers (reading 4.4.2); the fund answers to the host's parliament

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 73.

**Ostrom-Style Commons Governance: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/os.md#c4-4)

1. *Democratic accountability for ≥80% of major decisions*: short. short: within a commons the appropriators make and revise the rules (design principle 3), so its decisions are accountable; generalised to every common-pool resource it can govern, the commons employ a small share of an economy's workers and the mechanism governs no firm (reading 4.4.1)
2. *removal/replacement mechanisms functional*: out of reach. out of reach beyond the commons: within it monitors are accountable to the appropriators or drawn from them (design principle 4), but generalised it governs no removal of legislators, executives or managers (reading 4.4.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 73.

**Universal Basic Income: 0.5** (v1 0.0, up). [Evidence](evidence/ubi.md#c4-4)

1. *Democratic accountability for ≥80% of major decisions*: out of reach. out of reach: an unconditional income strengthens the power to refuse work (exit, C2.5's), but places no decision with those it affects (reading 4.4.1)
2. *removal/replacement mechanisms functional*: out of reach. out of reach: the mechanism governs no removal of legislators, executives or managers (reading 4.4.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 73.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.0. 0.5: a transfer mechanism that governs neither quantity (D28(c))

1. *Democratic accountability for ≥80% of major decisions*: not shown. on decision 48.3's measure as on part (b7)'s reading: the rationale names decentralisation, wealth caps, cooperative ownership and democratic governance, and degrowth research holds the forms of democracy compatible with degrowth to need additional study; no source states or projects the share of employment degrowth would place in organizations whose decisions answer to those they affect
2. *removal/replacement mechanisms functional*: not shown. as part (b7) recorded: no mechanism of the entry's own for removing or replacing those who hold decision-making authority is specified or located

2. *removal/replacement mechanisms functional*: out of reach. out of reach: the mechanism governs no removal of legislators, executives or managers (reading 4.4.2)

**Mutual Credit / LETS: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/mc.md#c4-4)

1. *Democratic accountability for ≥80% of major decisions*: out of reach. out of reach: the network's members govern the network and its credit limits, but it places no organization's investment, production or employment decisions (reading 4.4.1)
2. *removal/replacement mechanisms functional*: out of reach. out of reach: the mechanism governs no removal of legislators, executives or managers (reading 4.4.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 73.

**State Capitalism / Singapore (GLC Developmental Capitalism): 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/sg.md#c4-4)

1. *Democratic accountability for ≥80% of major decisions*: short. far short: Singapore is an electoral autocracy (Regimes of the World 1 throughout 2014 to 2025), so the public sector and the government-linked companies do not count; self-employment is 12.7% (reading 4.4.1)
2. *removal/replacement mechanisms functional*: short. short: multiparty elections are held and the opposition wins votes and seats (34.4% of the vote and the Workers' Party's 10 of 97 seats in 2025), but V-Dem does not rate them free and fair, and the party has governed since 1959 (reading 4.4.2)

Why 0.0 stands: protocol 2.1, read by reading 4.4.3: no free elections (V-Dem's electoral autocracy throughout 2014 to 2025) and no member control, with clause 1 far short (the public sector not counted, self-employment 12.7%)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 73.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5. 0.0: the blind reading's own 4.4.3 conditions hold

**Status Quo Market Capitalism: 0.5** (v1 0.0, up). [Evidence](evidence/sq.md#c4-4)

1. *Democratic accountability for ≥80% of major decisions*: short. far short on the most generous count: the United States is an electoral democracy in 2025 (Regimes of the World 2), so government counts, 14.8% of nonfarm payrolls; self-employment is 6.1%; nonprofits (about a tenth of private employment) and cooperatives (about 2 million jobs) counted in, the share stays near a third; the rest work in enterprises whose boards their owners elect (reading 4.4.1)
2. *removal/replacement mechanisms functional*: cleared. cleared: legislators and executives are removable in free multiparty elections (Regimes of the World 3 to 2024, 2 in 2025) (reading 4.4.2)

**Universal Basic Services: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/ubs.md#c4-4)

1. *Democratic accountability for ≥80% of major decisions*: short. short: generalised to every service it provides (health, education, care, housing, transport, information), its public and community providers answer to elected authorities where the host is a democracy, but they employ a minority of the workforce, far from 80%, and participatory governance is proposed, not specified (reading 4.4.1)
2. *removal/replacement mechanisms functional*: out of reach. out of reach: the mechanism governs no removal of legislators, executives or managers (reading 4.4.2); the services' governance is the host's

**Stakeholder Capitalism: 0.5** (v1 0.0, up). [Evidence](evidence/sc.md#c4-4)

1. *Democratic accountability for ≥80% of major decisions*: short. short: the reform widens boards' duties to stakeholders but leaves boards elected by owners; even parity codetermination gives the shareholder-side chair the casting vote, so the board answers to owners in the definition's sense (reading 4.4.1)
2. *removal/replacement mechanisms functional*: cleared. cleared, on the host's institutions, which the reform leaves unmodified: free elections remove legislators and executives (Regimes of the World 2, United States 2025) (reading 4.4.2)

**State Capitalism / China (Party-State-Directed Market Economy): 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/cn.md#c4-4)

1. *Democratic accountability for ≥80% of major decisions*: short. far short: China is a closed autocracy (Regimes of the World 0 throughout), so neither public administration nor the state-owned enterprises count; self-employment is 37.7% of employment in 2025 (falling from 41.0% in 2016), and the collectives' and enterprises' leaders are appointed through the Party's cadre system (reading 4.4.1)
2. *removal/replacement mechanisms functional*: short. short: no free elections; the Party's leadership is constitutional (article 1), and neither legislators nor executives can be removed by those they govern (reading 4.4.2)

Why 0.0 stands: the Party-state concentrates decision-making in a narrow group whose removal by those it governs its own constitution excludes, with clause 1 far short (reading 4.4.3)

**State Capitalism / Qatar (Gulf Rentier-Distributive Statism): 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/qa.md#c4-4)

1. *Democratic accountability for ≥80% of major decisions*: short. far short: Qatar is a closed autocracy (Regimes of the World 0 throughout), so the public sector does not count; self-employment is 0.8% of employment, the migrant majority of the workforce employed in owner-governed firms (reading 4.4.1)

**Doughnut Economics: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/de.md#c4-4)

2. *removal/replacement mechanisms functional*: not shown. not shown: the framework specifies no institutions of removal; its city portraits sit in elected municipal governments, which are the host's, not the design's (reading 4.4.2)

**Centrally Planned Socialism: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/cps.md#c4-4)

1. *Democratic accountability for ≥80% of major decisions*: short. far short: the Soviet Union was a closed autocracy (Regimes of the World 0 in every year from 1922 to 1990), so the state sector does not count; collective-farm chairmen were nominated by the district Party committee, enterprise directors appointed by their ministries, and private and cooperative work outside the collective farms was marginal until 1988 (reading 4.4.1)
