### C4.5 Exploitation Elimination

Pass Threshold (v2.0): Extraction rates <10% GDP, genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships. Revision class D (clauses deleted only).

v2.0: 3 at 1.0, 13 at 0.5, 7 at 0.0. v1: 6 at 1.0, 10 at 0.5, 7 at 0.0.

**CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/cco.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: short. short: Public Trust Housing turns 70-80% of a renter's payment into equity and Public Trust Foundations run essential businesses on a not-for-profit model, removing the owner's and landlord's share where they operate; the design coexists with private markets ('without ... the dismantling of private markets'), projects Public Trust Housing at 15-20% of the market within a decade, and taxes labour exploitation at a higher corporate rate rather than removing it (reading 4.5.1)
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: short. short: Basic Units, about $1,200 an adult a month, opt-in and restricted to essentials, and the pay-in pathway out of renting with no deposit or credit check, are real exits where they reach; at the projected reach of the housing trusts and with a floor near the poverty line, leaving an employer, landlord or lender is not assured for the population the design serves (reading 4.5.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 71.

**Nordic Social Democracy: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/nsd.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: short. far short: one minus the labour share of GDP is 38.0% in Denmark, 42.9% in Finland, 47.1% in Norway (40.9% net of resource rents) and 44.3% in Sweden (2019), and the labour share in the SDG series is 57.0%, 54.3% and 51.7% in 2024 (Denmark, Finland, Sweden): around the United States' 40.3%, Denmark a little below and the others above; collective bargaining, codetermination and tenant law regulate the terms of the three relationships, none is removed (reading 4.5.1)
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: short. short, but near: universal health care and social assistance do not depend on a job, unemployment insurance pays a voluntary quitter after a bounded suspension (in Sweden 45 benefit days, Lag 1997:238, 43 b), and tenants hold security of tenure; the floor is conditional on seeking work, so leaving wage employment as such carries a penalty (reading 4.5.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 71.

**Market Socialism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/ms.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: not shown. not shown: in worker cooperatives the surplus goes to the worker-members, removing the employer-employee transfer for them; rent to private landlords and interest to banks remain, which on US proportions would be about 5.7% of GDP, but the exemplar hires non-member workers in its subsidiaries, and the design projects no economy-wide rate (readings 4.5.1, 2.3(b))
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: short. short: a member may leave with the capital account repaid, and the group's mutual insurance pays unemployment support and relocates members between cooperatives; tenancy and credit have no exit beyond the market's (reading 4.5.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 71.

**Participatory Economics: 1.0** (v1 0.0, up). [Evidence](evidence/pe.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: cleared. cleared, projected: no private ownership of productive assets, so no profit, rent or interest income accrues to owners; households' budgets are an effort rating plus any allowance, and production and investment are set in the participatory plan (reading 4.5.1)
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: cleared. cleared: the design keeps no employer, landlord or creditor; besides the effort-rated budget it provides allowances for those excused from production and a consumption allowance for people who do not want to work, in effect an unconditional basic income (Wright's statement of the procedure, which Hahnel's reply keeps as effort rating plus any allowance), so subsistence does not depend on any relationship (reading 4.5.2, as part (b8) read Integral and FALC)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 71.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5.

**Integral: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/int.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: cleared. removes employer-employee extraction
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: cleared. the design has no employer, landlord or creditor: housing is community-controlled, production cooperative, and ITC credits cannot be borrowed, lent or accrue interest; a participant can leave a team or a node, contribution in one node is recognised for access in another, basic needs are accessible below standard contribution thresholds with a need-based adjustment for caregiving and health constraints, and a participant can leave the federation without penalty (reading 3.6)

Read in part (b8) (Session 49).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5. Read as requiring that a participant able to contribute who declines all contribution keep access to basic needs, the design does not show the clause: basic needs are accessible below standard contribution thresholds, which the design's sou

**Sovereign Wealth Fund Statism: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/swf.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: short. short: the fund holds a share of the capital stock and returns its income to the public, by budget rule or dividend, so part of the transfer to owners is publicly appropriated; the relationships in the economy it is layered onto and in the firms it owns are unmodified, and Norway's non-labour share is 47.1% of GDP (40.9% net of resource rents, 2019) (readings 4.5.1, 4.5.4)
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: short. short: Alaska's dividend is universal but small, Norway's model pays none; no exit from employment, tenancy or credit follows (reading 4.5.2)

Why 0.0 stands: protocol 2.1, first condition, read with reading 4.5.3: the fund removes no extractive relationship -- it neither regulates nor displaces the employment, tenancy and credit relations of the economy it is layered onto or of the firms it owns -- and redistributes part of the proceeds

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 71.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5. 0.0: the blind reading's own clause estimates found every relationship unmodified, which reading 4.5.3 scores 0.0

**Ostrom-Style Commons Governance: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/os.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: short. short: generalised, appropriators governing a common-pool resource pay no rent to an outside owner, and community land trusts (in scope, D31) remove the landlord's land rent for residents; the transfer to employers and lenders elsewhere in the economy is not governed (readings 4.5.1, 4.5.4)
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: short. short: a land-trust resident holds a long ground lease and leaves with a share of the equity; commoners hold membership; no floor or alternative for employment or credit (reading 4.5.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 71.

**Universal Basic Income: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/ubi.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: out of reach. the economy-wide extraction share is not governed by an income transfer
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: cleared. an exit option from exploitative work

Read in part (b8) (Session 49).

**Degrowth Economics: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/dg.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: cleared. removes profit motive for extraction
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: not shown. universal basic services secure housing, healthcare, education and transport, but for employment the entry specifies reduced hours and work-sharing, not an income floor or an assured alternative, and its own C2.2 rationale says the design does not fully decouple survival from employment, so exit from an employment relationship without penalty is not shown (reading 3.6)

Read in part (b8) (Session 49).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 1.0. Read as the entry's C2.1 rationale reads its universal basic services, as eliminating survival coercion, exit from employment would be genuine; the services it names cover housing, healthcare, education and transport, not food or income, an

**Georgism / Land Value Tax (LVT + Citizen's Dividend): 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/geo.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: short. short: generalised, the land value tax captures land rent up to its full rental value, removing the landlord's share of the land flow, but rent on buildings, profit and interest, which George's design treats as earned returns, stay at the host economy's level, far above 10% of GDP (readings 4.5.1, 4.5.4)
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: short. short: the citizen's dividend is unconditional, but the transfers located are a small fraction of a basic-needs budget, so leaving an employer, landlord or lender is not made survivable by it (reading 4.5.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 71.

**Islamic Finance / Profit-Sharing Banking: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/if.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: short. short: generalised, the prohibition of interest replaces the loan with profit-and-loss sharing and sale-based contracts, but wage employment and rent (ijara) are permitted, and the fixed-return modes (markup sale, tawarruq) are more than 70% of financing in Malaysia and the Middle East, so the lender's return continues in another form (readings 4.5.1, 4.5.4)
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: short. short: a debtor in hardship is owed respite and late-payment charges go to charity, a real limit on the creditor's hold; exit from employment and tenancy is the host's (reading 4.5.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 71.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.0. 0.5: profit-and-loss sharing is a genuine mechanism on the creditor-debtor relationship (reading 4.5.3)

**Mutual Credit / LETS: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/mc.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: short. short: generalised, credit created at zero balance and zero interest between members removes the lender's interest on the credit it replaces; the transfer to employers and landlords is not governed by the mechanism and stays the host's (readings 4.5.1, 4.5.4)
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: short. short: an interest-free credit line is an assured alternative to borrowing from a lender, within its limit, but it gives no exit from employment or tenancy (reading 4.5.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 71.

**State Capitalism / Singapore (GLC Developmental Capitalism): 0.5** (v1 0.0, up). [Evidence](evidence/sg.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: short. far short: one minus the labour share is 56.1% of GDP (2019), the highest of the configured economies after Qatar; the state owns most land and sells 99-year HDB leases at subsidised prices, so 77.2% of resident households (2025) pay no private landlord, while the transfer to the owners of government-linked and foreign firms and to lenders remains (reading 4.5.1)
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: short. short: residents may quit, with a jobseeker support scheme since 2025 for involuntary job loss only; Work Permit holders, about a quarter of the workforce, need the current employer's consent to transfer outside a window before the permit expires, and are outside HDB (reading 4.5.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 71.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.0. 0.5: HDB removes the private landlord for most residents, a structural mechanism on one relationship type (reading 4.5.3)

**Status Quo Market Capitalism: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/sq.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: short. far short: one minus the labour share is 40.3% of GDP (2019); on the narrow count corporate profits, rental income and net interest are 18.7% of GDP in 2025 (13.0%, 3.7% and 2.0%), up from 14.8% in 2000; each of the three relationships is in place and none is removed (reading 4.5.1)
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: short. far short: at-will employment gives the formal right to quit, but leaving is not survivable without another job: every state disqualifies a voluntary quit from unemployment insurance, 53.5% of people hold employment-based health insurance (2025), and tenancy and consumer credit carry no exit beyond the market's (reading 4.5.2)

Why 0.0 stands: protocol 2.1, third condition: performance far below the threshold on both clauses -- extraction four times the bar on the broad count and nearly twice on the narrow, and rising; exit no more than the formal right to quit -- with no rule or instrument in the configuration that removes an extractive relationship or moves the rate toward 10% (reading 4.5.3)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 71.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5.

**Universal Basic Services: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/ubs.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: short. short: generalised, housing, health, education and transport provided free or decommodified remove the landlord's rent for those housed and much borrowing for education and health; the transfer to employers is not governed (readings 4.5.1, 4.5.4)
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: short. short: services in kind make losing a job less ruinous but give no income, so leaving wage employment is not survivable on them alone (reading 4.5.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 71.

**MMT + Job Guarantee: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/mmt.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: short. short: the job guarantee's living wage sets a floor under private wages and raises workers' bargaining power; ownership, rent and lending stay the host's (readings 4.5.1, 4.5.4)
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: short. short: the guaranteed public job is an assured alternative to an extractive employer that is not itself extractive; tenancy and credit have none (reading 4.5.2)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 71.

**Fully Automated Luxury Communism: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/falc.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: cleared. Eliminates labor exploitation
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: cleared. the design provides universal material provision, housing included, through automated production and makes labour an optional hobby rather than a survival requirement, so no one depends on an employment, tenancy or credit relationship for subsistence (reading 3.6)

Read in part (b8) (Session 49).

**Stakeholder Capitalism: 0.0** (v1 0.5, down). [Evidence](evidence/sc.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: short. far short: the entry keeps shareholder ownership and wage employment and adds voluntary consideration of stakeholders and ESG metrics, which change neither the transfer to owners nor rent or interest; in the market economy it reforms the narrow count is 18.7% of GDP (reading 4.5.1)
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: short. far short: exit is the host market economy's, the formal right to quit with no floor added; the entry specifies no exit from employment, tenancy or credit (reading 4.5.2)

Why 0.0 stands: protocol 2.1, third condition: far below on both clauses with no pathway under the system's own logic -- the entry is voluntary corporate responsibility within the market framework, which leaves every extractive relationship in place, and Bebchuk and Tallarita (2020) found the 2019 Business Roundtable statement largely unaccompanied by changes in its signatories' corporate governance (reading 4.5.3)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 71.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5.

**State Capitalism / China (Party-State-Directed Market Economy): 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/cn.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: short. far short: one minus the labour share is 41.4% of GDP (2019); urban land is state-owned and rural land collective, but the state takes the land rent only as the lease premium at sale: the 70-year use right leaves later gains in land value, the bulk of two decades' rise, with private holders, landlords included; profit, interest to state banks and private housing rent remain, and the state's share of the surplus is extraction relocated (reading 4.5.1)
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: short. far short: a worker may quit, but unemployment insurance covers about a third of the employed (245.89 million insured, end of 2024), the minimum living guarantee is means-tested and thin, household registration ties services to place, no union independent of the Party may organise, and the US Department of Labor lists goods made with state-organised forced labour in Xinjiang (2022) (reading 4.5.2)

Why 0.0 stands: protocol 2.1, third condition: far below on both clauses -- the transfer to private and state owners in place, state-organised coerced labour among its forms, no independent bargaining -- with no rule or instrument in the configuration that removes an extractive relationship (reading 4.5.3)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 71.

**State Capitalism / Qatar (Gulf Rentier-Distributive Statism): 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/qa.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: short. far short: one minus the labour share is 82.4% of GDP (2019); net of natural-resource rents (22.4% of GDP) it is still 60.0%; the migrant majority's wages are set by a sponsorship market, and the rent the state captures is distributed to citizens, about an eighth of residents (reading 4.5.1)
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: short. far short for the population the protocol counts (residents, non-citizens included, protocol 3.2): since 2020 a migrant worker may change employer without a No Objection Certificate (348,450 changes, November 2020 to August 2022, against about 18,000 in 2019), but residence depends on employment, recruitment debt is widespread and the ILO records retaliation by employers; citizens' floor (public employment, housing loans) covers about 12% of residents (reading 4.5.2)

Why 0.0 stands: protocol 2.1, third condition: far below on both clauses for the residents the configuration counts -- the non-labour share is the corpus's highest even net of resource rents, the low-wage migrant majority is load-bearing for the configuration, and its exit stops at changing sponsor -- with no rule or instrument in the configuration that removes an extractive relationship (reading 4.5.3)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 71.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5.

**Libertarian Minarchism: 0.0** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/lm.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: short. short: no mechanism limits extraction; the design treats voluntary exchange as non-exploitative by definition and keeps every relationship type (employer and employee, landlord and tenant, lender and borrower)
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: short. short: exit is the formal right to quit, with no subsistence floor and no assured alternative

Why 0.0 stands: reading 4.5.3's 0.0 line (protocol 2.1): far below on both clauses, every relationship type in place, exit no more than the formal right to quit, and no rule or instrument that removes one, as for Status Quo

**Doughnut Economics: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/de.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: not shown. not shown: the framework's 'design to distribute' names redistribution of land, enterprise, money creation, technology and knowledge, with land value taxes, employee ownership and commons as examples, but fixes no mechanism and projects no rate (reading 4.5.1)

**Centrally Planned Socialism: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/cps.md#c4-5)

1. *Extraction rates <10% GDP*: short. surplus appropriated by the state rather than private owners: extraction continued, to another appropriator, with nothing placing it below 10% of GDP, as part (a) found
2. *genuine exit rights from exploitative relationships*: short. exit from employment was penalised: the 1977 Constitution made conscientious work the duty of every able-bodied citizen and declared evasion of socially useful work incompatible with socialist society, and the RSFSR's decree of 4 May 1961 on persons avoiding socially useful work punished it (reading 3.6)

Read in part (b8) (Session 49).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.0. State appropriation read as structural, load-bearing extraction, the 0.0 band's own test (part (a))

### C4.6 Harm Internalization

Pass Threshold (v2.0): Mortality attributed to pollution non-increasing over 20 years; those who cause harm to health or the environment liable for it, enforceably by those harmed; regulation of food, water, air, chemicals and medicines applied and enforced without improper influence (for a configured national economy, a World Justice Project sub-factor 6.2 score of 0.77 or more). Revision class N (new criterion).

v2.0: 0 at 1.0, 22 at 0.5, 1 at 0.0. New in v2.0.

**CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP: 0.5** (new in v2.0). [Evidence](evidence/cco.md#c4-6)

1. *Mortality attributed to pollution non-increasing over 20 years*: not shown. not shown: the sources project no pollution mortality for the economy they propose (reading 4.6.1)
2. *those who cause harm to health or the environment liable for it, enforceably by those harmed*: not shown. not shown: the sources state no liability of those who cause harm to those harmed (reading 4.6.2)
3. *regulation of food, water, air, chemicals and medicines applied and enforced without improper influence (for a configured national economy, a World Justice Project sub-factor 6.2 score of 0.77 or more)*: not shown. not shown: the CIP paper names regulatory capture (Stigler) as the problem it answers, with FDA and EPA among its examples, but provides for elected officials, campaign finance and the Judicial Guard, not for the bodies that regulate food, water, air, chemicals and medicines (reading 4.6.3)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 76.

**Nordic Social Democracy: 0.5** (new in v2.0). [Evidence](evidence/nsd.md#c4-6)

1. *Mortality attributed to pollution non-increasing over 20 years*: cleared. cleared in each of the four: DNK 51.3 to 23.5, FIN 19.1 to 7.7, NOR 28.0 to 8.3, SWE 23.2 to 7.4 per 100,000 (reading 4.6.1)
2. *those who cause harm to health or the environment liable for it, enforceably by those harmed*: not shown. not shown: the law in force makes polluters liable -- Denmark's Environmental Damage Compensation Act ('shall compensate the damage the pollution causes', section 3, save damage due to compliance with an authority's mandatory rules, a defence the clause may read as an immunity), Sweden's Environmental Code (chapter 32), Finland's Environmental Damage Compensation Act (liability 'even when the damage was not caused intentionally or negligently', section 7); Norway's act was not reached -- before courts Freedom House scores 4 of 4, but no claim decided against those who caused the harm and paid within the window is named in the cache for any of the four (reading 4.6.2)
3. *regulation of food, water, air, chemicals and medicines applied and enforced without improper influence (for a configured national economy, a World Justice Project sub-factor 6.2 score of 0.77 or more)*: cleared. cleared in each of the four: DNK 0.97, FIN 0.97, NOR 0.99, SWE 0.92, above 0.77 (reading 4.6.3)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 77.

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 1.0. 0.5: mortality falling, regulation clean, strict liability in force; no claim record named

**Market Socialism: 0.5** (new in v2.0). [Evidence](evidence/ms.md#c4-6)

1. *Mortality attributed to pollution non-increasing over 20 years*: not shown. not shown: the sources project no pollution mortality for the economy they propose (reading 4.6.1)
2. *those who cause harm to health or the environment liable for it, enforceably by those harmed*: not shown. not shown: the sources state no liability of those who cause harm to those harmed (reading 4.6.2); Roemer argues that public bads such as pollution would be set lower because the rich would not dominate the political decision, a political-economy argument, not a liability provision
3. *regulation of food, water, air, chemicals and medicines applied and enforced without improper influence (for a configured national economy, a World Justice Project sub-factor 6.2 score of 0.77 or more)*: not shown. not shown: the sources state no provision for the bodies that regulate food, water, air, chemicals and medicines or their independence (reading 4.6.3)

Read in group 2.11 (Session 65) and re-read blind in Session 76.

**Participatory Economics: 0.5** (new in v2.0). [Evidence](evidence/pe.md#c4-6)

1. *Mortality attributed to pollution non-increasing over 20 years*: not shown. not shown: the sources project no pollution mortality for the economy they propose (reading 4.6.1)
2. *those who cause harm to health or the environment liable for it, enforceably by those harmed*: cleared. cleared: a community of affected parties decides how much of each pollutant may be emitted, the emitting council pays the damage price as a cost of production, and the affected receive credit for the damage they suffer: a compensation scheme funded by those who cause the harm, collective and controlled by those harmed (reading 4.6.2)
3. *regulation of food, water, air, chemicals and medicines applied and enforced without improper influence (for a configured national economy, a World Justice Project sub-factor 6.2 score of 0.77 or more)*: not shown. not shown: the community of affected parties is advised by scientists in R&D units it runs, which is independence of the harmed from the harm-causers for pollution; regulation of food, water, chemicals and medicines is not addressed (reading 4.6.3)

1. *Mortality attributed to pollution non-increasing over 20 years*: not shown. not shown: the sources project no pollution mortality for the economy they propose (reading 4.6.1)

## Domain 5

<!-- generated:D5 -->
### C5.1 Proven Component Foundation

Pass Threshold (v2.0): ≥70% of system components proven through ≥20 years operation at scale ≥10,000 participants, with documented outcomes matching claimed benefits. Revision class W (wording, commentary or disclosure only).

v2.0: 2 at 1.0, 21 at 0.5, 0 at 0.0. v1: 13 at 1.0, 9 at 0.5, 1 at 0.0.

**CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/cco.md#c5-1)

1. *≥70% of system components proven through ≥20 years operation at scale ≥10,000 participants*: not shown. the design's own site lists five components (CCO, PTF, PTH, SZH, CIP); the precedents the rationale cites cover three (CCO: WIR and Alaska's dividend; PTF and PTH: community land trusts); CIP's precedents, Consul (Madrid, 2015) and Decidim (Barcelona, 2016), have run for under twenty years, and none is cited for SZH: at most three of five (60%) are shown (reading 3.2)
2. *with documented outcomes matching claimed benefits*: cleared. the one benefit the rationale claims for a cited component is documented: at the end of 2010, 0.46% of community land trust mortgages were in foreclosure against 4.63% in the conventional market; Alaska's dividend's documented poverty reduction is recorded under UBI C5.1 above

Read in part (b2) (Session 39).

**Nordic Social Democracy: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/nsd.md#c5-1)

1. *≥70% of system components proven through ≥20 years operation at scale ≥10,000 participants*: cleared. decades of operation in Norway, Sweden, Denmark and Finland at national scale (populations of 5-10 million)
2. *with documented outcomes matching claimed benefits*: not shown. the configuration's headline claims (near-universal material security; comprehensive security and dignity) are recorded short by the corpus's own amended scoring: housing cost overburden in 2024 at 14.6% in Denmark and 10.6% in Sweden, among the EU's highest (part (b), NSD C1.3), and about 6% of Finland's citizens' initiatives adopted (part (a), NSD C2.4); the rationale asserts validation without estimating a match (reading 3.1)

Read in part (b2) (Session 39).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 1.0. Outcomes matched against the configuration's signature claims, low poverty and universal services, with the housing and initiative shortfalls read as outcomes on NEEC's thresholds rather than on the benefits the model claims

**Market Socialism: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/ms.md#c5-1)

1. *≥70% of system components proven through ≥20 years operation at scale ≥10,000 participants*: cleared. Mondragon: 70+ years at a scale far above 10,000 participants
2. *with documented outcomes matching claimed benefits*: short. the rationale claims 80,000+ worker-owners; in 2019 Mondragon's cooperatives employed over 81,000, of whom members were 32-45% depending on sector, with 18% of employment in subsidiaries abroad; the survival record is documented as 3 closures among the 103 cooperatives founded in 1956-1986, and the founding cooperative, Fagor Electrodomesticos, failed in 2013

Read in part (b2) (Session 39).

**Participatory Economics: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/pe.md#c5-1)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Participatory budgeting exists in 3,000+ municipalities. Worker councils function in cooperatives. However, comprehensive parecon was never implemented nationally. Components proven separately but not as an integrated system.

**Integral: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/int.md#c5-1)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Individual components are well-evidenced — cybernetic theory (Ashby, Beer, the real if politically-terminated Project Cybersyn), cooperative organization (Mondragón's 70+ years and 97% five-year survival rate, 313 U.S. Community Land Trusts), and deliberation platforms (Decidim, Polis, Loomio) — but the document itself concedes the integration layer is untested: "None of this has to be invented from scratch. What needs to be built is the integration layer." No implementation of the full five-system architecture exists at any scale.

**Sovereign Wealth Fund Statism: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/swf.md#c5-1)

1. *≥70% of system components proven through ≥20 years operation at scale ≥10,000 participants*: cleared. Norway's fund since 1990 and Alaska's since 1976, both far above the scale bar
2. *with documented outcomes matching claimed benefits*: not shown. outcomes are mixed across the three implementations the rationale counts: Norway's fund matches its claims; Alaska's principal is preserved but its 2025 dividend was the smallest in real terms against a statutory formula above $3,800 (the entry's own C5.4); Timor-Leste has withdrawn above its sustainable income almost every year since 2008-09, and the IMF warns the fund could be exhausted by the late 2030s (reading 3.1)

Read in part (b2) (Session 39).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 1.0. Timor-Leste weighed as a single implementation's shortfall (D29(a)), with Norway's and Alaska's funds preserved as the mechanism claims

**Ostrom-Style Commons Governance: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/os.md#c5-1)

1. *≥70% of system components proven through ≥20 years operation at scale ≥10,000 participants*: cleared. Valencia's tribunal for about a thousand years; Nepal's programme with about 2.9 million households
2. *with documented outcomes matching claimed benefits*: cleared. the benefit claimed is that communities governed by these institutions sustain their resources: the three multi-case datasets the unit cites document it, and Cox, Arnold and Villamayor Tomas's review of 91 studies found the design principles well supported empirically; the survivorship caveat is scored where it changes the answer (C3.3, C5.2, C5.4), as the unit says

Read in part (b2) (Session 39).

**Universal Basic Income: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/ubi.md#c5-1)

1. *≥70% of system components proven through ≥20 years operation at scale ≥10,000 participants*: cleared. Alaska Permanent Fund dividend: 40+ years, universal
2. *with documented outcomes matching claimed benefits*: cleared. the rationale claims cash transfers effective with positive outcomes, and the Report claims a 20% poverty reduction from Alaska's dividend (UBI C1.1): dividends cut Alaska's poverty rate by an estimated 2.5-4 points a year from 1990, lifting about 25,000 residents out of poverty in 2015, about a third, and from 11% to 9% across 2011-2015; the dividend has not reduced employment

Read in part (b2) (Session 39).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5. The documented outcomes are of a dividend of about $1,000-2,000 a year; no component precedent operates at the living-wage level at which the entry claims its benefits

**Degrowth Economics: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/dg.md#c5-1)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Components proven: cooperatives, time banks, community land trusts, transition towns. However, comprehensive degrowth was never implemented at national scale. Piecemeal validation but not system-level.

**Georgism / Land Value Tax (LVT + Citizen's Dividend): 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/geo.md#c5-1)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> The individual components are unusually well-proven — LVT has 100+ years of continuous operation across multiple jurisdictions, and the Citizen's Dividend component has its own 40+-year precedent (Alaska PFD) already credited elsewhere in this Report. But the *specific* integration this evaluation scores — LVT as a primary revenue base paired with a universal dividend at near-single-tax intensity — has been tried and failed at least once in a directly relevant way: Britain's 1910 Land Value Duty (part of Lloyd George's "People's Budget") collapsed under valuation disputes and administrative costs reportedly running to roughly four times the revenue collected, and was eventually repealed.

**Islamic Finance / Profit-Sharing Banking: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/if.md#c5-1)

1. *≥70% of system components proven through ≥20 years operation at scale ≥10,000 participants*: cleared. every named contract in commercial use at scale since 1983
2. *with documented outcomes matching claimed benefits*: short. the design promises profit-and-loss sharing; debt-like sale contracts dominate (tawarruq 46% of Malaysian Islamic financing by 2019), and the document leaves the match to its other criteria

Read in part (a) (Session 37).

**Mutual Credit / LETS: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/mc.md#c5-1)

1. *≥70% of system components proven through ≥20 years operation at scale ≥10,000 participants*: cleared. WIR Bank has operated continuously since 1934
2. *with documented outcomes matching claimed benefits*: not shown. the components the rationale counts have mixed documented outcomes, as the entry's own document records: WIR's credit moves countercyclically (C3.1), but UK LETS declined from its mid-1990s peak once cheap credit returned, its schemes drew participants already socially connected (C1.1), and Argentina's barter networks collapsed as they outgrew their governance (C3.2, C3.5); WIR's 2013 turnover was 1.43 billion francs, and Bank WIR reports demand for the currency falling again in 2024 (reading 3.1)

Read in part (b2) (Session 39).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 1.0. WIR's documented record, nine decades of operation and countercyclical credit, taken as the proof of the mechanism's core component, with the LETS and barter-network outcomes read as weaker implementations

**State Capitalism / Singapore (GLC Developmental Capitalism): 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/sg.md#c5-1)

1. *≥70% of system components proven through ≥20 years operation at scale ≥10,000 participants*: cleared. operated since independence in 1965 at national scale
2. *with documented outcomes matching claimed benefits*: not shown. the rationale certifies track record, not adequacy (Status Quo's reasoning), and cites Domains 1 and 3 for its documented outcomes, which the corpus's own scoring records short: C1.1 0.5, housing affordability not maintained for the rental segment (part (a), SG C1.3), and evidence-based updates slow or refused (part (a), SG C3.4) (reading 3.1)

Read in part (b2) (Session 39).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 1.0. Outcomes matched against the narrow functions its sources claim for residents, home ownership and compulsory saving

1. *≥70% of system components proven through ≥20 years operation at scale ≥10,000 participants*: cleared. operated for centuries; its components in continuous national-scale operation far beyond 20 years
2. *with documented outcomes matching claimed benefits*: not shown. the rationale claims longevity, not a match of outcomes to claimed benefits, and concedes the record does not validate adequacy for contemporary challenges; the corpus's own scoring of this entry records outcomes short of the broad-prosperity benefits its defenders claim (C1.1 0.5; C1.2b, C1.4, C4.4 0.0)

1. *≥70% of system components proven through ≥20 years operation at scale ≥10,000 participants*: not shown. the document defines seven sectors (healthcare, education, democracy and legal services, shelter, food, transport, information); its cited precedents of 20 years or more cover three (healthcare, education, shelter), its childcare precedent lies outside the seven, and it calls its transit precedents more recent and more mixed: three of seven (43%) are shown (reading 3.2)

**Doughnut Economics: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/de.md#c5-1)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> The diagnostic and planning component — the City Portrait methodology and its underlying indicator sets — is genuinely well-proven at real-world scale: piloted in three cities in 2019, adopted in some form by more than 50 local and regional governments and one national government by 2025, and, as of October 2025, anchored by a peer-reviewed publication in one of the world's most selective scientific journals. This is a strong, multi-year, multi-jurisdiction record for the specific thing being proven. But the fuller "economic system" NEEC evaluates requires more than a diagnostic apparatus, and that fuller doughnut economy — one that actually closes the gaps the diagnostic tool identifies — has not been achieved, or even attempted as an integrated whole, anywhere: the framework's own 2025 monitoring finds the global economy further from the doughnut than in 2000, not closer. The redistributive components that would be needed (Domain 1) are each separately proven elsewhere (as this project's own Georgism and Mutual Credit/LETS evaluations already establish) but are not part of what has actually been integrated into any Doughnut Economics implementation. *(continues in the scoring document)*

> Historical existence proves technical feasibility but not desirability. 70+ years of operation in the USSR, continued existence in Cuba and North Korea. However, the collapse of the Eastern Bloc demonstrates lack of sustained viability.

### C5.2 Staged Transition Pathways

Pass Threshold (v2.0): Staged (≥4 phases) and rapid (≤36 months) deployment, with specific milestones, resource requirements and risk mitigation, each shown feasible by precedent or component evidence. Revision class M (bar, measure or clause restated).

v2.0: 2 at 1.0, 19 at 0.5, 2 at 0.0. v1: 7 at 1.0, 13 at 0.5, 3 at 0.0.

**CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/cco.md#c5-2)

1. *Staged (≥4 phases)*: not shown. the roadmap specifies seven phases over seven years, but its second phase launches the dual currency for all Americans in year 2, and its phases deploy components of which no deployment at comparable scale is located, a national complementary currency whose units expire monthly and convert at merit-based rates, and a national portal for blockchain-verified voting and direct policy proposals (the nearest located are local, Wörgl's stamp scrip of 1932-1933, or partial, Estonia's internet voting in national elections since 2007); the modelling paper's pilot-first pathway has three phases, municipal pilots, regional expansion and national implementation, and concludes that the computational evidence supports proceeding with municipal pilot programs to begin empirical validation (reading 3.4)
2. *rapid (≤36 months) deployment*: not shown. the three-month national launch plan and the Report's 18-36 months are plans; the precedents the entry cites, the New Deal's and the Marshall Plan's three years, are programmes of other kinds cited for their speed; component evidence covers the design's universal distribution (Alaska's dividend, paid to residents every year since 1982), but no deployment of a national expiring currency or of binding national digital democracy, within 36 months or at any pace, is located (reading 3.4)
3. *specific milestones*: not shown. the milestones (poverty below 10% in year 1 and below 2% by year 7, enrolment of 80% in year 1, 10,000 Creator Collectives in year 1; the launch-phase KPIs) are the design's own targets; no precedent or component evidence is cited or located for them, and the modelling paper's phase success probabilities (84%, 78% and 71%) come from its own parameter sensitivity, a model outcome (reading 3.4)
4. *resource requirements*: not shown. the resources are stated ($500 billion over five years for Public Trust Foundations; in the three-month plan, $950 billion of federal allocations and some 190,000 staff and coordinators), but no precedent or component evidence shows them sufficient for the deployment: the documents cite none, and the modelling paper's fiscal break-even is a model outcome (reading 3.4)
5. *risk mitigation*: cleared. the plan's risk section and the Risk Mitigation Framework specify mitigations of kinds in use elsewhere (redundant systems and manual backups, audits, independent oversight bodies, algorithmic transparency), and the modelling paper's mitigation of transition disruption is the parallel operation of existing systems: component evidence in the sense of reading 3.2 (reading 3.4)

Read in part (b9) (Session 50).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 1.0. Read as part (a) read the Session 44 clause, on the plans' specificity, with the precedents the entry cites for their timing (the New Deal, the Marshall Plan) and the modelling paper's success probabilities as evidence of feasibility, every

**Nordic Social Democracy: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/nsd.md#c5-2)

1. *Staged (≥4 phases)*: cleared. the audited phrase, occurred gradually over 40+ years, names the configuration's own deployment, a precedent in the sense of the scope; it was built in successive stages in each country, in Sweden alone at least four: crisis policy and the Saltsjöbaden Agreement (1932-1938), the universal pension and child allowance (1946-1948), compulsory health insurance and the earnings-related pension (1955-1960), and parental insurance and the Co-determination Act (1974-1976) (reading 3.2)
2. *rapid (≤36 months) deployment*: not shown. the entry states no rapid pathway, and its own history is a build of four decades; its universal social insurance has a precedent of rapid deployment elsewhere (Britain's, legislated in 1946 and in force by July 1948), but the entry names strong labor unions as defining, and no deployment of encompassing unions and centralised bargaining within 36 months is located: Sweden's took from the December Compromise of 1906 to the Saltsjöbaden Agreement of 1938 (reading 3.3)
3. *specific milestones*: not shown. not estimated in this pass; the verdict is fixed by clause 2
4. *resource requirements*: not shown. not estimated in this pass; the verdict is fixed by clause 2
5. *risk mitigation*: not shown. not estimated in this pass; the verdict is fixed by clause 2

Read in part (b9) (Session 50).

**Integral: 0.5** (v1 0.0, up). [Evidence](evidence/int.md#c5-2)

1. *Staged (≥4 phases)*: not shown. the design's white paper, published after the corpus scored it, now states a staged pathway: proto-nodes grown from existing mutual aid networks, timebanks, tool libraries and repair cafés, each of the five systems formalised in three steps, and an arc of four stages, from marginal experiment to supplementary alternative, essential support layer and dominant substrate, 'inherently generational' and undated; its first stage's kind is deployed, but no node operating the design's systems is located: the project's site registers four communities in its own Phase 1, the building of its software (reading 3.3)
2. *rapid (≤36 months) deployment*: not shown. the white paper sets aside direct implementation in a nation-state as 'highly unlikely' and states no rapid pathway; none is located (reading 3.3)
3. *specific milestones*: not shown. no milestones are stated: the transition's markers are 'incremental and often unremarkable' (reading 3.3)
4. *resource requirements*: not shown. no resource requirements are stated; interface cooperatives take in external resources, without a figure (reading 3.3)
5. *risk mitigation*: cleared. section 11.10 specifies safeguards of kinds in use elsewhere: no single point of failure, peer-to-peer and federated infrastructure with replicated data, grounded in deployed technologies (decentralised identifiers, content-addressed storage, federated messaging); no central treasury; commons assets that cannot be pledged or sold; legal compliance as the default; and interface cooperatives with capped market exposure and sunset rules; component evidence in the sense of reading 3.2, as CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP's risk mitigation was cleared in part (b9) (reading 3.3)

Read in group 2.6 (Session 58).

**Sovereign Wealth Fund Statism: 1.0** (v1 0.5, up). [Evidence](evidence/swf.md#c5-2)

1. *Staged (≥4 phases)*: cleared. the mechanism's own deployment, in at least four dated stages in Norway: the fund's law (1990), its first capital transfer (1996), the investment manager and a 40% equity share (1998), and the fiscal rule tying spending to the fund's expected real return (2001) (reading 3.4)
2. *rapid (≤36 months) deployment*: cleared. a precedent of the same kind in a post-crisis context: Timor-Leste, independent in 2002 after the violence of 1999, put its petroleum fund out to public consultation (comments due 30 November 2004), promulgated its law on 3 August 2005 and made the fund's first investments on 9 September 2005, 10 months from the consultation's close (reading 3.4)
3. *specific milestones*: cleared. dated targets were set and met: a 40% equity share decided in 1997 and reached in the first half of 1998; 60% decided in 2007 and reached in June 2009; the fiscal rule's expected return revised from 4% to 3% in 2017 (reading 3.4)
4. *resource requirements*: cleared. what the deployments took is documented: the capital, the revenue stream transferred into the fund (in Timor-Leste, USD 79.6 million from the Timor Gap Account at the first investment), and the institution to manage it, built in Timor-Leste with Norway's assistance from 2002, about NOK 56 million to 2007, whose advisers were 'instrumental to the establishment of the petroleum fund and its operations' (reading 3.4)
5. *risk mitigation*: cleared. the deployments' mitigations operated: assets invested abroad only, a spending rule tying transfers to the expected real return (Norway) or to 3% of petroleum wealth (Timor-Leste), ethical guidelines from 2004; Norway's fund has been kept and has grown to more than four times mainland GDP under the rule, though Timor-Leste's parliament approved withdrawals above its rule in most years from 2008 (reading 3.4)

Read in group 2.6 (Session 58).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5. Every clause is shown by deployments of the mechanism itself, which the published 0.5, judged on the absence of a phase-by-phase plan, did not credit; under v2.0's scope a precedent, not a plan, is the test (part (b9), reading 3.2)

**Islamic Finance / Profit-Sharing Banking: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/if.md#c5-2)

1. *Staged (≥4 phases)*: cleared. the audited phrase, forty-year build, from the first Islamic bank in 1983, names a precedent: Malaysia's dual system was built in dated stages, among them the Islamic Banking Act and the first Islamic bank (1983), the interest-free banking scheme for conventional banks (1993), the Shariah Advisory Council (1997) and the Islamic Financial Services Act (2013) (reading 3.3)
2. *rapid (≤36 months) deployment*: cleared. the audited phrase names a precedent within the window: Iran legislated the conversion of its banking system in 1983 and operated the converted system from March 1984 (reading 3.3)
3. *specific milestones*: cleared. the audited phrase, milestones are real enough to be missed, is the precedent's milestone record: Malaysia set a 50% Islamic share by 2025 and measured 48%, so its milestones were dated and checked (reading 3.3)
4. *resource requirements*: not shown. the entry states no resource requirement for either pathway, and the precedents it cites document laws, dates and shares, not the resources the conversions took (capital, trained staff, liquidity instruments); reports of Pakistan's 2026 roadmap say that banks' Islamic windows already hold the technology and that staff training is under way, but give no estimate of cost, capital or staff (reading 3.3)
5. *risk mitigation*: cleared. the audited phrase, explicit grandfathering of existing conventional contracts, is Pakistan's measure, announced for 2028 and not yet in force; mitigation of the same kind has worked in the staged precedent: Malaysia's dual system required no forced conversion of existing contracts, running Islamic and conventional banking side by side for four decades (reading 3.3)

**Centrally Planned Socialism: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/cps.md#c5-2)

1. *Staged (≥4 phases)*: not shown. no gradual transition to comprehensive central planning in a stable context is located: every transition to the configuration followed a seizure of power by force, by revolution and civil war (Russia, 1917-1921; China, 1949), by occupation (Eastern Europe, 1945-1949) or by a coup (Czechoslovakia, February 1948); the later stages of each, the five-year plans and collectivisation, rest on it (readings 3.2(a), 3.5)
2. *rapid (≤36 months) deployment*: not shown. the configuration was imposed within 36 months more than once (Soviet collectivisation from 1929; the nationalisations in Eastern Europe after 1945), but each deployment rested on a seizure of power by force, which the criterion's rationale names as what a pathway is to avoid, and none was a deployment a polity could adopt by its own procedures (readings 3.2(a), 3.5)
3. *specific milestones*: not shown. the five-year plans set dated targets, but in deployments that rest on force; no milestone of a pathway the criterion credits is located (reading 3.5)
4. *resource requirements*: not shown. no statement of what a transition into the configuration takes, other than the seizure of power, is located (reading 3.5)
5. *risk mitigation*: not shown. the entry's record shows rapid implementation 'invariably chaotic', and no mitigation of a transition's risks is stated or located (reading 3.5)

Why 0.0 stands: every transition to the configuration located required non-gradual means, revolution, civil war, a coup or occupation (the 0.0 band's own example); no pathway the criterion credits exists to be shown feasible

### C5.3 Partial and Parallel Deployability

Pass Threshold (v2.0): Viable at ≥30% participation, coexistence with traditional markets maintaining ≥90% economic activity, scaling pathway validated through modeling, coordination protocols established. Revision class U (unchanged).

v2.0: 7 at 1.0, 14 at 0.5, 2 at 0.0. v1: 15 at 1.0, 6 at 0.5, 2 at 0.0.

**CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/cco.md#c5-3)

1. *Viable at ≥30% participation*: short. short on the preponderance of the design's own texts (protocol 4.2): the modelling paper finds 'minimum viable participation at 55%' ('below 55% participation rate, network effects fail to sustain system growth'), the BLEI paper a self-funding threshold of 60-65%, the sensitivity analysis 55-60%, and the model warns below 55%; the integrated framework's 'viability at 30%' states no computation
2. *coexistence with traditional markets maintaining ≥90% economic activity*: cleared. Can coexist with traditional markets
3. *scaling pathway validated through modeling*: cleared. Scaling pathway validated through modeling
4. *coordination protocols established*: cleared. Inter-jurisdictional coordination protocols specified

Read in group 2.5 (Session 57), then lowered to the adjudication's reading by the owner's decision 84.2 (ledger D67, Session 84).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 1.0. The integrated framework's reading: viability at 30% participation

**Nordic Social Democracy: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/nsd.md#c5-3)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Components can be adopted piecemeal (universal healthcare, free education, progressive taxation), but a comprehensive model requires near-universal participation for funding sustainability. Partial deployment is possible but less effective.

**Market Socialism: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/ms.md#c5-3)

3. *scaling pathway validated through modeling*: not shown. no modelling of a scaling pathway cited; observed cooperative scale peaks at a regional federation (Mondragon: about 70,000 workers in 92 cooperatives), short of the national stage the Measurement line describes (reading 3.4)
4. *coordination protocols established*: not shown. not estimated in this pass; the verdict is fixed by another clause

Read in part (b1) (Session 38).

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

**Integral: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/int.md#c5-3)

1. *Viable at ≥30% participation*: cleared. the federated structure starts with a handful of people
2. *coexistence with traditional markets maintaining ≥90% economic activity*: cleared. nodes coexist with traditional markets
3. *scaling pathway validated through modeling*: not shown. not shown: the white paper says thresholds 'are derived from: empirical system modeling, ecological science, historical performance data', how they are to be set, not a model validating the path from pilot to regional to national scale; Jev reads the clause short in both passes

Read in part (a) (Session 37), then adjudicated after the outside review (Session 83).

**Sovereign Wealth Fund Statism: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/swf.md#c5-3)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Definitionally and empirically clean. Every implementing jurisdiction operates independently of every other, and every one coexists with, rather than displaces, an ordinary market economy — this is simply the observed pattern across every exemplar located (Norway, Alaska, Timor-Leste, New Zealand, and dozens of others tracked by the Sovereign Wealth Fund Institute), not a projection requiring modeling. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**Ostrom-Style Commons Governance: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/os.md#c5-3)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Satisfied by construction and by observation. Commons institutions exist **inside** market economies and always have; there is no version of this mechanism that requires the market to be displaced first. Up to 2.5 billion people, at least 50% of the Earth's land customarily held collectively, and roughly one third of the world's forests under community-based, participatory or decentralised management — all coexisting with markets that carry the overwhelming majority of measured economic activity. The scaling pathway is principle 8 itself, nested enterprises, and it is validated rather than proposed: Nepal scaled from nothing to more than 22,000 user groups inside four decades. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**Universal Basic Income: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/ubi.md#c5-3)

1. *Viable at ≥30% participation*: cleared. implementable at municipal, state or national scale
2. *coexistence with traditional markets maintaining ≥90% economic activity*: cleared. Alaska shows coexistence with the market economy
3. *scaling pathway validated through modeling*: cleared. cleared on observed operation, at least as strong as modelling (protocol 2.3(d)): pilots of thousands; Alaska's dividend at state scale since 1982; and since December 2010 a universal, unconditional monthly cash transfer per person that nearly every resident of Iran receives
4. *coordination protocols established*: moot. moot: a single jurisdiction adopts the mechanism without coordination with others (Alaska, Iran), as Georgism's and Sovereign Wealth Fund Statism's clause 4 are read (protocol 2.3(e))

Read in part (a) (Session 37), then adjudicated after the outside review (Session 83).

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5. Iran's transfer compensates for removed price subsidies rather than paying an income as of right (Tabatabai), and no adequate basic income has been scaled

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Components can be implemented locally (transition towns, eco-villages). However, comprehensive degrowth requires systemic change. Difficult to maintain growth and degrowth economies simultaneously without degrowth being overwhelmed.

**Georgism / Land Value Tax (LVT + Citizen's Dividend): 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/geo.md#c5-3)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Every real-world case demonstrates this directly: a single city, a single sub-national territory (the ACT), or a single country (Estonia, Denmark) can adopt LVT independently, coexisting normally with an ordinary market economy and with neighboring jurisdictions that have not adopted it. No case of adoption requiring simultaneous multi-jurisdiction coordination was located — possibly the single most strongly-evidenced criterion in this entire evaluation.

**Islamic Finance / Profit-Sharing Banking: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/if.md#c5-3)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> The dual system is not an argument for partial deployability; it is a four-decade demonstration of it. Islamic and conventional banking operate side by side in Malaysia with no co-mingling of funds, the Islamic side at 48% of financing; in most other jurisdictions the mechanism operates at far lower shares without difficulty; conventional banks run Islamic windows; and sovereign issuers outside the traditional markets, including the United Kingdom, Luxembourg, and Hong Kong, have issued sukuk. The scaling pathway from a single licensed bank to near-parity is documented rather than modelled. Not flagged.

**Mutual Credit / LETS: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/mc.md#c5-3)

3. *scaling pathway validated through modeling*: short. the entry's own document records a practical trust ceiling of 100-200 members per LETS node and WIR's centralisation under scale; the federated redesign meant to pass them is not yet validated
4. *coordination protocols established*: moot. no case of adoption requiring or attempting multi-jurisdiction coordination

### C5.4 Political Coalition Potential

Pass Threshold (v2.0): ≥60% support across political spectrum, ≥65% opposition to repeal, survival probability ≥80% across administration changes. Revision class U (unchanged).

v2.0: 1 at 1.0, 19 at 0.5, 3 at 0.0. v1: 2 at 1.0, 17 at 0.5, 4 at 0.0.

**CCO-PTF-PTH-SZH-CIP: 0.5** (v1 1.0, down). [Evidence](evidence/cco.md#c5-4)

1. *≥60% support across political spectrum*: cleared. Alaska PFD demonstrates 80%+ approval across the spectrum
2. *≥65% opposition to repeal*: not shown. the design's documents state no projection of opposition to its repeal; its roadmap assumes full bipartisan political support with legislative supermajorities and a public mandate through 70%+ approval ratings, an assumption, not an estimate; the Alaska dividend's record is one component's, and the design's other components (the expiring currency, Public Trust Foundations, the portal, the zones) have none (reading 3.5)
3. *survival probability ≥80% across administration changes*: not shown. no record or projection of the design's survival across changes of administration is stated; the modelling paper puts political resistance in 34% of its scenarios and national implementation's success at 71%, neither of which is survival across administrations (reading 3.5)

Read in part (b9) (Session 50).

**Nordic Social Democracy: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/nsd.md#c5-4)

1. *≥60% support across political spectrum*: cleared. High public satisfaction (70-85%)
2. *≥65% opposition to repeal*: cleared. in the ISSP's 2016 Role of Government module, {SPEND} of those answering in each of the four countries wanted government spending on health, and on old age pensions, kept at its level or raised, and {RESP} held that health care for the sick and a decent standard of living for the old should be the government's responsibility; wanting the system's core provision kept or raised opposes its repeal a fortiori (reading 3.5)
3. *survival probability ≥80% across administration changes*: cleared. the configuration's universal social insurance and public services have been kept through every change of government since they were built, including centre-right governments in all four countries: Sweden 1976-1982, 1991-1994, 2006-2014 and from 2022; Denmark 1982-1993, 2001-2011 and 2015-2019; Norway 1981-1986, 1989-1990, 1997-2000, 2001-2005 and 2013-2021; Finland 1991-1995, 2015-2019 and from 2023 (reading 3.5)

Read in part (b9) (Session 50).

**Georgism / Land Value Tax (LVT + Citizen's Dividend): 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/geo.md#c5-4)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> The cross-ideological intellectual case is unusually strong: historical supporters span Winston Churchill, William F. Buckley Jr., and Milton Friedman on the right to Bertrand Russell, John Dewey, and Paul Krugman on the left. Currently, a 2026 survey found majority support for a specific pending LVT bill among both renters and homeowners in New York State, and Detroit's pending LVT plan is backed by 83% of a surveyed panel of economists including four Nobel laureates, projected to cut taxes for roughly 97% of the city's homeowners. Against this: Detroit's plan remains stalled in the Michigan Legislature despite this expert backing, the broader current U.S. property-tax political environment favors cutting or eliminating property taxes generally rather than restructuring their base toward land value, and Altoona, Pennsylvania's 2016 reversal is a direct instance of an actual implementation losing its political coalition.

**Islamic Finance / Profit-Sharing Banking: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/if.md#c5-4)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Support is durable where it exists: four decades of consistent Malaysian policy across changes of government, and a Pakistani constitutional amendment passed with the required two-thirds majority in both houses. But the coalition is bounded. In secular and non-Muslim-majority polities the mechanism is adopted as an instrument — sukuk issuance, window operations — rather than as an economic programme, and within the tradition it faces real scholarly opposition, with AAOIFI reservations about organised *tawarruq* and Middle Eastern rejection of structures Malaysia permits. H.7's 0.5 band — "appeals strongly to part of the spectrum while facing real resistance from another part" — applies. *(continues in the scoring document)*

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 1.0.

**Mutual Credit / LETS: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/mc.md#c5-4)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> The theoretical cross-ideological case is real — appeal to both libertarian-leaning mutual-aid values and left communitarian/degrowth-adjacent values — and WIR Bank has operated 90 years without evident political controversy. Against this, a real, repeated, cross-context pattern of friction with state authority: UK benefits-eligibility treatment (C2.1), Kenyan authorities briefly detaining Grassroots Economics organizers in 2013 over concerns Bangla-Pesa undermined the national currency before charges were dropped, and Argentina's own network founders facing arrest during the Trueque system's later troubles — a genuine, structural tension with states' legal monopoly on currency issuance, not isolated anecdotes. Separately, UK LETS's own well-documented decline by the 2000s–2010s is attributed in part to the widespread availability of cheap conventional credit reducing the perceived need for an alternative.

**State Capitalism / Singapore (GLC Developmental Capitalism): 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/sg.md#c5-4)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Domestic support is strong and repeatedly tested at the ballot box. The PAP's vote share was 60.1% in 2011 (its lowest since independence), 69.9% in 2015, 61.24% in 2020, and 65.57% in 2025 (Malay Mail; The Asia Group). Trust in government stood at 77% in 2024 and rose during the pandemic (Civil Service College, citing Edelman). *(continues in the scoring document)*

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 1.0. Six decades of contested majorities as cross-spectrum support

**Status Quo Market Capitalism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/sq.md#c5-4)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

**Universal Basic Services: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/ubs.md#c5-4)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Real, current, and substantial evidence exists on both sides of this criterion's threshold. In favor: the UK Labour Party's 2019 conference named UBS one of three central pillars of its economic program (alongside ownership/democracy reform and a cash safety net), a genuine endorsement by a major political party's leadership; the concept has maintained sustained institutional traction at the international level, with IGP's own researchers serving as continuing lead authors on the G7's Think7 social-cohesion policy briefs across at least three separate presidency cycles (Germany 2022, Japan 2023, and into the 2026 cycle); and the underlying *principle* — universal, free-at-point-of-use, publicly delivered essential services — commands strong and, as of 2024–2026, *rising* cross-partisan UK public support for individual sectors specifically (YouGov polling finds 76% support for rail nationalization, including 60% of Conservative and 77% of Reform UK voters; 71% support for energy nationalization, up from 53% in 2017; and separate polling finds 86–90% support for keeping the NHS in public, free-at-point-of-use form). Against a stronger score: this polling measures support for individual, already-familiar sectors rather than the specific "Universal Basic Services" branded comprehensive package, which has not itself been polled as a whole to this evaluation's knowledge; Labour's 2019 enthusiasm predates that party's loss in the 2019 general election, so this evidence should be read as historical political-coalition proof-of-concept rather than confirmation of continuing current party policy; and Kansas City's own 2026 reversal of its zero-fare transit program (funding shortfalls following the exhaustion of pandemic-era federal relief funds, and documented year-over-year increases in bus-operator assaults and reported disturbances) is a real, recent, concrete illustration that even a comparatively narrow, single-sector UBS-adjacent policy can lose its political and fiscal coalition once initial funding conditions change. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**MMT + Job Guarantee: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/mmt.md#c5-4)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Appeals to progressives, labor unions, Keynesian economists. However, "printing money" rhetoric triggers inflation fears among fiscal conservatives. Mixed coalition potential.

2. *≥65% opposition to repeal*: not shown. not shown: no measure of opposition to repeal

But support across a political spectrum cannot be observed where parties are banned, the media is not independent, and most residents have no political rights (Freedom House). Outside Qatar, the configuration draws criticism from several directions: human-rights monitors on labour and guardianship, climate analysts on emissions, and the Heritage Foundation on government spending and reliance on expatriate labour (The Peninsula, 2025). **Doughnut Economics: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/de.md#c5-4)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> The framework's actual, dated adoption record spans a genuinely wide political and institutional range — a Swedish municipality, a UK county council, a Belgian regional government, a national government in Central America, cities across North America, Europe, Australasia, and, as of 2025–2026, a village-level local government in India (the Maniyur Gram Panchayat, which incorporated Doughnut Economics into its 2026–27 annual budget) — evidence of real cross-context appeal that a purely ideological or single-constituency framework would not show. Against this, a real and recurring durability problem: of the three cities that piloted the model together in 2019, an academic overview found that "only Amsterdam continues to work with the model," with Philadelphia's and Portland's engagement not sustained — direct evidence that symbolic or initial adoption is easier to achieve than sustained operational commitment. A doctoral case study of Cornwall Council separately documents real tension between local political and economic realities and the ambitions of Raworth's own vision once a jurisdiction moves from adopting the framework to implementing it. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**Centrally Planned Socialism: 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/cps.md#c5-4)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> **STRUCTURAL FAILURE.** Catastrophic historical record eliminates political viability. Post-1989 collapse of communism, recognition of authoritarian nature, economic dysfunction make democratic coalition building impossible.

### C5.5 Cultural Adaptability

Pass Threshold (v2.0): Viable across ≥3 economic contexts (high/middle/low income), ≥5 cultural contexts, parameter flexibility ≥40% adjustment range, successful operation validated across diverse implementations. Revision class U (unchanged).

v2.0: 3 at 1.0, 18 at 0.5, 2 at 0.0. v1: 4 at 1.0, 15 at 0.5, 4 at 0.0.

**Sovereign Wealth Fund Statism: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/swf.md#c5-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Real, if narrower-than-typical, diversity of successful contexts was located: a wealthy Nordic democracy (Norway), a US state with its own distinct politics (Alaska), a small, high-income, non- resource-funded Pacific democracy (New Zealand), and a genuinely low-income, small, post-conflict Southeast Asian state (Timor-Leste) have each built a working version of this mechanism. But nearly every large-scale, long-running success case shares one structural precondition this evaluation could not find a clean workaround for: a large, lumpy, non-recurring revenue source — a resource windfall in most cases, or a sustained, politically-durable general budget contribution in New Zealand's rarer case — to seed and sustain the fund. Even that rarer, non-resource case shows real fragility: New Zealand's own government contributions were suspended for roughly a decade (contributions resuming only from 2020/21 after a multi-year pause) as they competed against other budget priorities, and at least one 2026 critical analysis argues the fund now returns more to the Crown in tax than the Crown contributes to it annually — a specific, sourced critique this evaluation treats as one contested viewpoint rather than settled fact, but one pointing at a real structural tension: a windfall-funded model doesn't compete with existing spending commitments the way a contribution funded from ordinary revenue does. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**Ostrom-Style Commons Governance: 1.0** (v1 1.0). [Evidence](evidence/os.md#c5-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> **The strongest C5.5 case in the corpus, and the one criterion on which this entry scores above CCO-PTF-CIP-SZH.** The 1.0 is joint-best, shared with Participatory Economics, Mutual Credit / LETS and Islamic finance. Validated implementations span Swiss alpine pasture, Japanese irrigation and *iriai* forest commons, the Spanish *huerta*, Philippine *zanjera* irrigation, the Nepali middle hills, the Maine lobster fishery, Latin American artisanal shellfisheries, Mexican community forestry, East African and Latin American forests, and Indonesian *sasi* and *panglima laot* — across subsistence, middle-income and advanced market economies. Gutiérrez et al. state the breadth explicitly: 130 fisheries across countries at different development levels, ecosystems, fishing sectors and resource types. Cox et al. add 91 studies and Chhatre and Agrawal 80 commons across three continents. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**State Capitalism / China (Party-State-Directed Market Economy): 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/cn.md#c5-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> Working versions exist in culturally proximate Vietnam (C5.1). The clearest cross-cultural test, Ethiopia, made rapid progress on nearly every available socioeconomic indicator and launched a series of mega-projects. It then saw mass protests from 2014 and an unraveling of elite cohesion after Prime Minister Meles Zenawi's death in 2012, and the ruling coalition was dissolved in 2019 (*Ethiopia's "Developmental State": Political Order and Distributive Crisis*, open-access book, University of Manchester Global Development Institute, 2023). The model contains no explicit mechanism for adapting to different cultural contexts. *(continues in the scoring document)*

**State Capitalism / Qatar (Gulf Rentier-Distributive Statism): 0.0** (v1 0.0). [Evidence](evidence/qa.md#c5-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> The rentier-distributive configuration needs a precondition it cannot produce: resource rents that are very large relative to the citizen body. Rentier-state theory, from Beblawi and Luciani's distinction between allocation and production states onward, links such rents to states that secure consent through welfare rather than taxation (as summarised in a 2021 review; see also Gray's theory of "late rentierism," Georgetown University in Qatar, 2011). But rents do not reliably produce distribution. Oman and Equatorial Guinea had similar resource rents per person, a little over US$8,000 in 1995–2014, yet illustrate opposite outcomes, generosity and neglect (*American Political Science Review*, 2023, citing Ross). The configuration's successes are small, high-rent Gulf monarchies: one regional and cultural context. *(continues in the scoring document)*

Close call: a reasonable scorer could set 0.5. SWF Statism precedent, GCC variation

**Doughnut Economics: 0.5** (v1 0.5). [Evidence](evidence/de.md#c5-5)

Held at the published score; no unit of v2.0 read it. The v1 rationale:

> The geographic reach is real but skewed. A 2025 academic survey of 50 local governments working with Doughnut Economics found the following regional breakdown outside Europe: North America, 6; Asia, 5; Latin America and the Caribbean, 4; Australia and New Zealand, 3; and Sub-Saharan Africa, 1 — implying that roughly three-fifths of the sample is concentrated in Europe specifically, consistent with a separate bibliometric finding that the UK leads academic research on the topic, followed by the USA and other European countries. Costa Rica's national-scale application and the emergence of examples in Malaysia (Ipoh), India (the Maniyur panchayat), and parts of Latin America are genuine, if still modest, evidence of reach beyond wealthy, Western contexts — broader than Georgism's own evidence base, though not as deep into low-income contexts as Mutual Credit/LETS's Kenyan example. No systematic parameter-adjustment guidance for different income contexts (comparable to CCO-PTF's own explicit high/middle/low-income parameters) was located. *(continues in the scoring document)*
